## statutes

### Adult Abuse Act: definitions

- **id**: rsmo-455-010
- **cite**: RSMo 455.010
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2021 (the current version on revisor.mo.gov as of 2026-09-30; 7 histories). Governs the 1/27/2026 and 5/12/2026 offense dates and the 2025-2026 order-of-protection events.

(1) "Abuse", includes but is not limited to the occurrence of any of the following acts, attempts or threats against a person who may be protected pursuant to this chapter, except abuse shall not include abuse inflicted on a child by accidental means by an adult household member or discipline of a child, including spanking, in a reasonable manner: [...] (5) "Domestic violence", abuse or stalking committed by a family or household member, as such terms are defined in this section; [...] (6) "Ex parte order of protection", an order of protection issued by the court before the respondent has received notice of the petition or an opportunity to be heard on it; [...] (7) "Family" or "household member", spouses, former spouses, any person related by blood or marriage, persons who are presently residing together or have resided together in the past, any person who is or has been in a continuing social relationship of a romantic or intimate nature with the victim, and anyone who has a child in common regardless of whether they have been married or have resided together at any time; [...] (8) "Full order of protection", an order of protection issued after a hearing on the record where the respondent has received notice of the proceedings and has had an opportunity to be heard; [...] (10) "Pending", exists or for which a hearing date has been set; [...] (15) "Stalking", is when any person purposely engages in an unwanted course of conduct that causes alarm to another person, or a person who resides together in the same household with the person seeking the order of protection when it is reasonable in that person's situation to have been alarmed by the conduct. As used in this subdivision:


Defines the words every order-of-protection case turns on: abuse (assault, battery, coercion, harassment, sexual assault, unlawful imprisonment, abusing a pet), domestic violence, household member (includes anyone who has a child in common), ex parte vs. full order, petitioner, respondent, and stalking. A 'full order of protection' is defined as one issued after a hearing on the record where the respondent had notice and an opportunity to be heard.

- **elements**: Not an offense; definitional. A full order must be 'issued after a hearing on the record where the respondent has received notice of the proceedings and has had an opportunity to be heard' (455.010(8)) - the notice theme in ED114714 and 26MR-CR00472.
**annotations**

### Order of protection: hearings, duration, renewal, service

- **id**: rsmo-455-040
- **cite**: RSMo 455.040
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2021 (current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-09-30; 5 histories). Subsections 1-5 only.

(2) Upon motion by the petitioner, and after a hearing by the court, the full order of protection may be renewed annually and for a period of time the court deems appropriate, and unless the court at an evidentiary hearing made specific written findings that the respondent poses a serious danger to the physical or mental health of the petitioner or of a minor household member of the petitioner, the renewed protective order may be renewed periodically and shall be valid for at least one hundred eighty days and not more than one year from the expiration date of the previously issued full order of protection. If the court has made specific written findings that the respondent poses a serious danger to the physical or mental health of the petitioner or of a minor household member of the petitioner, the renewed protective order may be renewed periodically and shall be valid for at least two years and up to the life of the respondent. [...] (3) The court may, upon finding that it is in the best interest of the parties, include a provision that any full order of protection shall be automatically renewed for any term of renewal of a full order of protection as set forth in this section unless the respondent requests a hearing by thirty days prior to the expiration of the order. If for good cause a hearing cannot be held on the motion to renew or the objection to an automatic renewal of the full order of protection prior to the expiration date of the originally issued full order of protection, an ex parte order of protection may be issued until a hearing is held on the motion. When an automatic renewal is not authorized, upon motion by the petitioner, and after a hearing by the court, the second full order of protection may be renewed for an additional period of time the court deems appropriate, except that the protective order shall be valid for any term of renewal of a full order as set forth in this section. For purposes of this subsection, a finding by the court of a subsequent act of domestic violence, stalking, or sexual assault is not required for a renewal order of protection. [...] 2. The court shall cause a copy of the petition and notice of the date set for the hearing on such petition and any ex parte order of protection to be served upon the respondent as provided by law or by any sheriff or police officer at least three days prior to such hearing. The court shall cause a copy of any full order of protection to be served upon or mailed by certified mail to the respondent at the respondent's last known address. Notice of an ex parte or full order of protection shall be served at the earliest time, and service of such notice shall take priority over service in other actions, except those of a similar emergency nature. Failure to serve or mail a copy of the full order of protection to the respondent shall not affect the validity or enforceability of a full order of protection.


A full order is valid 180 days to one year (2 to 10 years with specific written 'serious danger' findings). Renewal: 'upon motion by the petitioner, and after a hearing by the court,' renewal is annual, running from the expiration date of the prior full order (455.040.1(2)). The court may include an auto-renewal clause that operates unless the respondent requests a hearing 30 days before expiration (455.040.1(3)); if a hearing cannot be held before expiration, an ex parte order may issue until the hearing. For a petition, service of the petition and hearing notice must occur at least three days before the hearing (455.040.2). Failure to serve or mail the full order does not affect its validity.

- **elements**: Full order: petitioner proves abuse/stalking/sexual assault by a preponderance and respondent cannot show the acts were justified (1)(1).; Renewal: (a) motion by petitioner; (b) hearing by the court (1)(2); no new act of abuse need be found for a renewal (1)(3).; Auto-renewal clause: court finding that it is in the parties' best interest; respondent's right to request a hearing 30 days before expiration (1)(3).

### Order of protection: contents of full/ex parte orders; custody and support terms

- **id**: rsmo-455-050
- **cite**: RSMo 455.050
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Revisor shows an 'Effective 28 Aug 2026' version (7 histories; L. 2026 H.B. 2273 et al.). The 6/23/2026 judgment predates it, so the version effective 8/28/2021 governs (earlier pipeline checked that the custody clause 455.050.3(1) is identical in both). The 2021 version text was NOT re-read in this task.

3. When the court has, after a hearing for any full order of protection, issued an order of protection, it may, in addition: [...] (1) Award custody of any minor child born to or adopted by the parties when the court has jurisdiction over such child and no prior order regarding custody is pending or has been made, and the best interests of the child require such order be issued; [...] 5. In making an award of custody, the court shall consider all relevant factors including the presumption that the best interests of the child will be served by placing the child in the custody and care of the nonabusive parent, unless there is evidence that both parents have engaged in abusive behavior, in which case the court shall not consider this presumption but may appoint a guardian ad litem or a court-appointed special advocate to represent the children in accordance with chapter 452 and shall consider all other factors in accordance with chapter 452.


Lists what a protective order may enjoin and, after a hearing on a full order, what ancillary relief the court may add: custody of a child 'born to or adopted by the parties' when it has jurisdiction and no prior custody order is pending or made and 'the best interests of the child require such order be issued' (from the 2021 version as quoted by the earlier pipeline); visitation; child support; maintenance; property; counseling; pets.  Text shown is the CURRENT (8/28/2026) version.

- **elements**: Custody term: court has jurisdiction over the child; no prior custody order pending or made; best interests of the child require the order; child born to or adopted by the parties.

3. No order entered pursuant to sections 455.010 to 455.085 shall be res judicata to any subsequent proceeding, including, but not limited to, any action brought under chapter 452. [...] 6. The order of protection may not change the custody of children when an action for dissolution of marriage has been filed or the custody has previously been awarded by a court of competent jurisdiction.


Orders can be modified; custody/support/visitation terms terminate on a later Chapter 452 order; an order of protection is not res judicata in later proceedings; it may not change custody when a dissolution action is pending or custody was previously awarded by a court of competent jurisdiction.

### Mootness: public-interest exception for expired full orders

- **id**: rsmo-455-007
- **cite**: RSMo 455.007
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2014 (2 histories); current 2026-09-30.

455.007. Mootness doctrine, public interest exception to apply upon expiration of the order. — Notwithstanding any other provision of law to the contrary, the public interest exception to the mootness doctrine shall apply to an appeal of a full order of protection which has expired.


Makes the public-interest exception to mootness apply to an appeal of a full order of protection that has expired, so the ED114714 appeal should not be dismissed merely because the order expires 6/23/2027.

### Violation of order of protection: arrest and penalties

- **id**: rsmo-455-085
- **cite**: RSMo 455.085
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2022 (6 histories); current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-09-30. Governs 1/27/2026 (26MR-CR00032 Count I) and 5/12/2026 (26MR-CR00472).

7. A violation of the terms and conditions, with regard to domestic violence, stalking, sexual assault, child custody, communication initiated by the respondent or entrance upon the premises of the petitioner's dwelling unit or place of employment or school, or being within a certain distance of the petitioner or a child of the petitioner, of an ex parte order of protection of which the respondent has notice, shall be a class A misdemeanor unless the respondent has previously pleaded guilty to or has been found guilty in any division of the circuit court of violating an ex parte order of protection or a full order of protection within five years of the date of the subsequent violation, in which case the subsequent violation shall be a class E felony. Evidence of prior pleas of guilty or findings of guilt shall be heard by the court out of the presence of the jury prior to submission of the case to the jury. If the court finds the existence of such prior pleas of guilty or finding of guilt beyond a reasonable doubt, the court shall decide the extent or duration of sentence or other disposition and shall not instruct the jury as to the range of punishment or allow the jury to assess and declare the punishment as a part of its verdict. [...] 8. A violation of the terms and conditions, with regard to domestic violence, stalking, sexual assault, child custody, communication initiated by the respondent or entrance upon the premises of the petitioner's dwelling unit or place of employment or school, or being within a certain distance of the petitioner or a child of the petitioner, of a full order of protection shall be a class A misdemeanor, unless the respondent has previously pleaded guilty to or has been found guilty in any division of the circuit court of violating an ex parte order of protection or a full order of protection within five years of the date of the subsequent violation, in which case the subsequent violation shall be a class E felony. Evidence of prior pleas of guilty or findings of guilt shall be heard by the court out of the presence of the jury prior to submission of the case to the jury. If the court finds the existence of such prior plea of guilty or finding of guilt beyond a reasonable doubt, the court shall decide the extent or duration of the sentence or other disposition and shall not instruct the jury as to the range of punishment or allow the jury to assess and declare the punishment as a part of its verdict. For the purposes of this subsection, in addition to the notice provided by actual service of the order, a party is deemed to have notice of an order of protection if: [...] 10. Nothing in this section shall be interpreted as creating a private cause of action for damages to enforce the provisions set forth herein.


Violating listed terms of an ex parte order (subsection 7) or a full order (subsection 8) is a class A misdemeanor, or a class E felony if the respondent previously pleaded guilty to or was found guilty of violating an ex parte or full order within five years of the subsequent violation. For subsection 8, notice is shown by actual service, by an officer presenting a copy, or by actual communication reasonably likely to advise the respondent. Subsection 4 gives good-faith immunity to arresting officers from false arrest/false imprisonment/malicious prosecution suits. Subsection 10: no private damages action.

- **elements**: (Case law, Schleiermacher) A valid court order was in effect prohibiting the conduct.; The order had been served on or read to the defendant, or notice exists under 455.085.8(1)-(2).; The defendant violated a listed term (abuse, stalking, sexual assault, child custody, respondent-initiated communication, entering the petitioner's dwelling/work/school premises, or being within a stated distance).; Enhancement (class E felony): prior plea/finding of guilt for violating an order within five years of the subsequent violation, found by the court beyond a reasonable doubt outside the jury's presence.

### Assault in the fourth degree

- **id**: rsmo-565-056
- **cite**: RSMo 565.056
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 1/1/2017 (revisor 'all versions', bid=29283; text read from that version) governs the 1/27/2026 offense. A version effective 7/1/2027 (L. 2026 H.B. 2637 & 3155 merged with S.B. 999) replaces 'physical injury, physical pain, or illness' with 'bodily harm' and is what revisor.mo.gov's default page displays; it does NOT govern.

1. A person commits the offense of assault in the fourth degree if: [...] (1) The person attempts to cause or recklessly causes physical injury, physical pain, or illness to another person; [...] (3) The person purposely places another person in apprehension of immediate physical injury; [...] (5) The person knowingly causes or attempts to cause physical contact with a person with a disability, which a reasonable person, who does not have a disability, would consider offensive or provocative; or [...] (6) The person knowingly causes physical contact with another person knowing the other person will regard the contact as offensive or provocative. [...] 2. Except as provided in subsection 3 of this section, assault in the fourth degree is a class A misdemeanor. [...] 3. Violation of the provisions of subdivision (3) or (6) of subsection 1 of this section is a class C misdemeanor unless the victim is a special victim, as the term "special victim" is defined under section 565.002, in which case a violation of such provisions is a class A misdemeanor.


Six ways to commit fourth-degree assault: attempting to cause or recklessly causing physical injury, physical pain, or illness; criminally negligent injury by firearm; purposely placing another in apprehension of immediate physical injury; recklessly creating a substantial risk of death or serious physical injury; offensive/provocative contact with a person with a disability; and knowing offensive contact. Generally a class A misdemeanor; subdivisions (3) and (6) are class C unless the victim is a 'special victim' (565.002), which includes a law enforcement officer assaulted in the performance of official duties, in which case class A.

- **elements**: Subdivision (1): attempts to cause, or recklessly causes, physical injury, physical pain, or illness to another person.; Subdivision (6): knowingly causes physical contact with another person knowing the other will regard it as offensive or provocative.; Grading: class A misdemeanor; subdivisions (3)/(6) are class C unless the victim is a special victim (law enforcement officer in official duties), then class A.
- **related**: RSMo 565.002; RSMo 562.011; RSMo 494.480; MAI-CR 4th 419.16-419.18 (numbers not verified)
- **definedTerms**: physical injury (not defined on the page read); special victim; reckless; voluntary act; class A misdemeanor
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032
- **cautions**: VERSION TRAP: use the pre-7/1/2027 text ('physical injury, physical pain, or illness'); the default revisor page shows 'bodily harm'. The 7/1/2027 text was also read and differs in subdivisions (1), (2), (3), (4).; Warrant (Evid. b1b3) lists Count III under 'Subdivision (1), (5)'; subdivision (5) concerns a person WITH A DISABILITY, which does not fit a police corporal - probable clerical error to raise. The First Amended Information (8/17/2026) was unread by the earlier pipeline and may have changed this.; Probable-cause statement reportedly says the strike 'did not cause injury, it caused several seconds of pain' (case-file Note); pain alone satisfies subdivision (1) in the version in force.; Do not draft a physical-contact theory until Tyler resolves decision D-1 (flat denial vs involuntary contact during a fall); the two are mutually exclusive.; MAI-CR 4th numbers come from a public-defender table dated 7-1-18; instruction text not retrieved.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=565.056&bid=29283
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Chapter 565 definitions (incl. 'special victim')

- **id**: rsmo-565-002
- **cite**: RSMo 565.002
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2017 (bid=34703, 4 histories; 'Special victim' is subdivision (14)) read from revisor 'all versions'. The default revisor page now displays a version effective 7/1/2027 (special victim at subdivision (16); adds 'bodily harm' definitions) which does not govern 2026 offenses.

(14) "Special victim", any of the following:
(a) A law enforcement officer assaulted in the performance of his or her official duties or as a direct result of such official duties;


Defines 'special victim' to include a law enforcement officer assaulted in the performance of official duties, which raises class C fourth-degree assault subdivisions (3)/(6) to class A. Also defines 'great bodily harm' and 'substantial bodily harm' in the 2027 version.

- **related**: RSMo 565.056
- **definedTerms**: special victim; conduct; course of conduct
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032

- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=565.002&bid=34703

### Resisting or interfering with arrest, detention, or stop

- **id**: rsmo-575-150
- **cite**: RSMo 575.150
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2025 (A.L. 2025 H.B. 495; 5 histories). Governs the 1/27/2026 offense. Pre-2025 text is a known drafting defect; do not reuse.

1. A person commits the offense of resisting or interfering with arrest, detention, or stop if he or she knows or reasonably should know that a law enforcement officer is making an arrest or attempting to lawfully detain or stop an individual or vehicle, and for the purpose of preventing the officer from effecting the arrest, stop or detention, he or she: [...] (1) Resists the arrest, stop or detention of such person by using or threatening the use of violence or physical force or by fleeing from such officer; or [...] 2. This section applies to: [...] 4. It is no defense to a prosecution pursuant to subsection 1 of this section that the law enforcement officer was acting unlawfully in making the arrest. However, nothing in this section shall be construed to bar civil suits for unlawful arrest. [...] 5. The offense of resisting or interfering with an arrest is a class E felony for an arrest for a: [...] The offense of resisting an arrest, detention or stop in violation of subdivision (1) or (2) of subsection 1 of this section is a class A misdemeanor, unless the person fleeing creates a substantial risk of serious physical injury or death to any person, in which case it is a class E felony.


Offense: a person who knows or reasonably should know a law enforcement officer is making an arrest or attempting to lawfully detain or stop an individual or vehicle and, for the purpose of preventing the officer from effecting it, (1) resists by using or threatening violence or physical force or by fleeing, or (2) interferes with another person's arrest/stop by violence, physical force or physical interference. Applies with or without warrant and to any offense, infraction or ordinance violation. Subsection 4: it is no defense that the officer acted unlawfully, but civil suits for unlawful arrest are not barred. Class E felony for certain felony/felony-warrant arrests; otherwise a class A misdemeanor (E felony if fleeing creates a substantial risk of serious physical injury or death).

- **elements**: Knows or reasonably should know a law enforcement officer is making an arrest or attempting to lawfully detain or stop.; Purpose of preventing the officer from effecting the arrest, stop or detention.; Resists by using or threatening the use of violence or physical force, or by fleeing (subdiv. 1) / interferes with another's arrest by violence, force or physical interference (subdiv. 2).
**annotations**

#### State v. Shaw (reporter cite as printed in revisor annotation), 592 S.W.3d 354 (Mo. banc 2019)

- **court**: Mo. Sup. Ct. (en banc)
- **year**: 2019
- **holding**: Revisor annotation: felony resisting does not require evidence of the arresting officer's subjective state of mind. Docket SC97605 exists; the printed reporter cite did not resolve.
- **verification**: unverified
- **treatment**: Citation lookup returned NOT FOUND on CourtListener (2026-09-30); the same case exists under docket SC97605 (filed 2019-12-10).


- **related**: RSMo 562.011; RSMo 565.056; 42 U.S.C. 1983; MAI-CR 4th 429.60/429.61 (numbers not verified)
- **definedTerms**: resisting arrest; purpose (mental state); voluntary act; unlawful arrest (no defense)
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 1983 pipeline (held; 4:26-cv unfiled)
- **cautions**: 575.150.4: the unlawfulness of the arrest is NOT a defense to the criminal charge (standing correction); keep that theory for the civil/1983 case. The subsection expressly preserves civil suits for unlawful arrest.; Remaining defense themes (purpose element; voluntary act under 562.011; whether going limp/to the ground is 'violence or physical force') have no Missouri authority located under the 2025 text.; Revisor annotation State v. Shaw predates the 2025 amendment and its reporter cite (592 S.W.3d 354) did not resolve on CourtListener (docket SC97605 does).; Heck risk: a conviction on Count II could bar 1983 claims that imply its invalidity; dismissal/acquittal changes that (see 42 U.S.C. 1983 entry).
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=575.150
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Tampering in the first degree

- **id**: rsmo-569-080
- **cite**: RSMo 569.080
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 1/1/2017 (2 histories; A.L. 2014 S.B. 491); current 2026-09-30. Governs 26RL-CR00078.

1. A person commits the offense of tampering in the first degree if he or she: [...] (2) Knowingly receives, possesses, sells, or unlawfully operates an automobile, airplane, motorcycle, motorboat or other motor-propelled vehicle without the consent of the owner thereof. [...] 2. Upon a finding by the court that the probative value outweighs the prejudicial effect, evidence of the following is admissible in any criminal prosecution of a person under subdivision (2) of subsection 1 of this section to prove the requisite knowledge that he or she: [...] 3. The offense of tampering in the first degree is a class D felony.


First-degree tampering has two routes; subdivision (2) makes it a class D felony to knowingly receive, possess, sell, or unlawfully operate a motor vehicle 'without the consent of the owner thereof.' Subsection 2 lets the court admit, after a probative-versus-prejudice finding, evidence of prior unlawful receipt/possession/operation or acquiring the vehicle for a price the person knew was far below its reasonable value.

- **elements**: Subdivision (2): knowingly receives, possesses, sells, or unlawfully operates an automobile or other motor-propelled vehicle.; Without the consent of the owner.
- **related**: RSMo 569.010; RSMo 569.090; RSMo 562.011; RSMo 610.140; RSMo 610.122
- **definedTerms**: tamper (569.010(5)); owner; consent; knowingly
- **matters**: 26RL-CR00078; 26RL-CV00280; 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053
- **cautions**: The offense was dismissed 8/26/2026 (with or without prejudice unconfirmed); State reportedly plans to refile by about January 2027. Statements Tyler makes under oath in 26PI-CC00044/26PI-CC00053 can be used in a refiled case (Fifth Amendment exposure).; Subdivision (2) ties the offense to 'the consent of the owner'. The case file's theory (Tyler is the titled owner) would defeat the element as a matter of law, but the HPD report paraphrases Tyler as saying he 'gifted' the vehicle (disputed) and Lake holds a signed title - ownership is contested.; The 'to tamper' definition in 569.010(5) applies to 569.090(1)(1), not to subdivision (2) of this section, which uses 'receives, possesses, sells, or unlawfully operates'.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=569.080
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

(5) "To tamper", to interfere with something improperly, to meddle with it, displace it, make unwarranted alterations in its existing condition, or to deprive, temporarily, the owner or possessor of that thing;


Defines 'to tamper' as interfering with something improperly, meddling with it, displacing it, making unwarranted alterations in its existing condition, or depriving, temporarily, the owner or possessor of that thing. ### Tampering in the second degree

- **id**: rsmo-569-090
- **cite**: RSMo 569.090
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2016 (3 histories; text also shows 'Effective 1-01-17'); current 2026-09-30.

1. A person commits the offense of tampering in the second degree if he or she: [...] (1) Tampers with property of another for the purpose of causing substantial inconvenience to that person or to another; or [...] (2) Unlawfully rides in or upon another's automobile, airplane, motorcycle, motorboat or other motor-propelled vehicle; or [...] 3. Tampering in the second degree is a class A misdemeanor unless:


Second-degree tampering: tampering with property of another for the purpose of causing substantial inconvenience; unlawfully riding in or upon another's motor vehicle; utility tampering. Class A misdemeanor unless enhanced; a prior conviction or finding under subdivision (2) makes it a class D felony.

### Voluntary act

- **id**: rsmo-562-011
- **cite**: RSMo 562.011
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 1/1/2017 (2 histories); current 2026-09-30.

1. A person is not guilty of an offense unless his or her liability is based on conduct which includes a voluntary act. [...] 2. A "voluntary act" is: [...] (1) A bodily movement performed while conscious as a result of effort or determination; or [...] (2) An omission to perform an act of which the actor is physically capable. [...] 3. Possession is a voluntary act if the possessor knowingly procures or receives the thing possessed, or having acquired control of it was aware of his or her control for a sufficient time to have enabled him or her to dispose of it or terminate his or her control.


No criminal liability without conduct that includes a voluntary act: a bodily movement performed while conscious as a result of effort or determination, or an omission to perform an act of which the actor is physically capable. Possession is voluntary if knowingly procured/received or, having acquired control, the possessor was aware of the control long enough to dispose of it.

- **elements**: A voluntary act or omission (conscious effort or determination), or knowing possession.
- **related**: RSMo 575.150; RSMo 565.056; RSMo 569.080
- **definedTerms**: voluntary act; possession
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26RL-CR00078
- **cautions**: No Missouri pattern instruction on voluntary act located for Count II; no authority located applying 562.011 to contact during a fall. The defense is an argument, not a settled rule.; Subsection 3 (knowing possession) matters to the tampering 'possesses' route in 569.080.1(2).
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=562.011
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Speedy trial (statutory request)

- **id**: rsmo-545-780
- **cite**: RSMo 545.780
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1986; current 2026-09-30.

1. If defendant announces that he is ready for trial and files a request for a speedy trial, then the court shall set the case for trial as soon as reasonably possible thereafter. [...] 2. The provisions of this section shall be enforceable by mandamus. Neither the failure to comply with this section nor the state's failure to prosecute shall be grounds for the dismissal of the indictment or information unless the court also finds that the defendant has been denied his constitutional right to a speedy trial.


If the defendant announces ready for trial and files a request for a speedy trial, the court shall set the case for trial 'as soon as reasonably possible thereafter.' Enforceable by mandamus. Neither non-compliance nor the State's failure to prosecute is a ground for dismissal unless the court also finds a denial of the defendant's constitutional right to a speedy trial. There is NO day-count in the statute.

- **elements**: Defendant announces ready for trial AND files a request for a speedy trial (subsection 1).; Remedy: trial setting as soon as reasonably possible; mandamus; dismissal only with a constitutional speedy-trial violation (subsection 2).
**annotations**

#### State ex rel. McKee v. Riley, 240 S.W.3d 720 (Mo. banc 2007)

- **court**: Mo. Sup. Ct. (en banc)
- **year**: 2007
- **holding**: Statute 'supplements the constitutional right'; represented defendant cannot personally invoke it (counsel must announce ready); dismissal only with a constitutional violation; enforceable by mandamus (text read).
- **verification**: full-text
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 48 as of 2026-09-30)

#### State v. Owsley, 959 S.W.2d 789 (Mo. banc 1997)

- **court**: Mo. Sup. Ct. (en banc)
- **year**: 1997
- **holding**: Dismissal unavailable absent constitutional violation (as cited in McKee).
- **verification**: existence-only
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 56 as of 2026-09-30)

#### Barker v. Wingo, 407 U.S. 514 (1972)

- **court**: U.S. Supreme Court
- **year**: 1972
- **holding**: Four-factor constitutional test.
- **verification**: existence-only
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 12229 as of 2026-09-30); the four factors were seen quoted in McKee

#### State v. Williams, 34 S.W.3d 440 (Mo. App. S.D. 2001)

- **court**: Mo. Ct. App. S.D.
- **year**: 2001
- **holding**: Eight-month delay presumptively prejudicial (reported via McKee).
- **verification**: existence-only
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 12 as of 2026-09-30)


- **related**: Rule 20.01; RSMo 544.676 (not read)
- **definedTerms**: speedy trial; Barker factors; mandamus; presumptive prejudice
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: NOT a dismissal vehicle: subsection 2 bars dismissal on a statutory violation alone; the 'drafting defects to not re-use' Note lists 545.780 as a dismissal vehicle. Use it only as a request/mandamus statute, and the constitutional analysis (Barker) for any dismissal theory.; The earlier 1/8/2027 (180 days after ~7/12/2026) and 2/21/2027 (180 days after 8/25/2026) dates do not trace to any rule or statute; 2/21/2027 is a Sunday. No fixed-day out-on-bond misdemeanor deadline was found. 'Rule 27.40(b)' does not exist (Rule 27 runs 27.01-27.26).; McKee concerned a REPRESENTED defendant's pro se motions; Tyler has been pro se since MSPD withdrew 6/8/2026, but whether his 7/13/2026 demand 'announced ready' is unverified (text not in the record).; The 120-day right in RSMo 544.676.3 applies only to a defendant denied bail after a written request at arraignment; Tyler posted a $7,500 bond 1/30/2026 (not read here).; Re-verified 2026-10-05 on revisor.mo.gov (subsection 1 quote and subsection 2 no-dismissal rule).

### Peremptory challenges

- **id**: rsmo-494-480
- **cite**: RSMo 494.480
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 7/1/1997; current 2026-09-30.

2. In all criminal cases, the state and the defendant shall be entitled to a peremptory challenge of jurors as follows: [...] (3) In all cases not punishable by death or imprisonment in the penitentiary, the state and the defendant shall each have the right to challenge two.


In criminal cases not punishable by death or imprisonment in the penitentiary, the state and the defendant each have two peremptory challenges (six each where penitentiary time is possible; nine each in capital cases). In civil cases each party has three, with multiple plaintiffs/defendants joining as one side.

- **related**: RSMo 455.085; Rule 27.05 (not quoted)
- **definedTerms**: peremptory challenge
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Class A misdemeanor trials: 2 strikes (not 3). If the State amends 472 to a class E felony under 455.085.8 enhancement (penitentiary-punishable), the count would rise to six each.; Civil trials (26PI-CC00044/-53): three per side (494.480.1).
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=494.480
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Peace disturbance

- **id**: rsmo-574-010
- **cite**: RSMo 574.010
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 1/1/2017 (2 histories); current 2026-09-30.

1. A person commits the offense of peace disturbance if he or she: [...] (1) Unreasonably and knowingly disturbs or alarms another person or persons by: [...] (c) Threatening to commit a felonious act against any person under circumstances which are likely to cause a reasonable person to fear that such threat may be carried out; or [...] 2. The offense of peace disturbance is a class B misdemeanor upon the first conviction. Upon a second or subsequent conviction, peace disturbance is a class A misdemeanor. Upon a third or subsequent conviction, a person shall be sentenced to pay a fine of no less than one thousand dollars and no more than five thousand dollars.


Peace disturbance: unreasonably and knowingly disturbing or alarming others by loud noise, face-to-face offensive language likely to produce an immediate violent response, threatening a felonious act under circumstances likely to cause fear, fighting, or noxious odor; or purposely obstructing traffic/ingress. Class B misdemeanor on first conviction.

- **elements**: Unreasonably and knowingly disturbs or alarms another by one of the listed means; or purposely causes inconvenience by unreasonably obstructing traffic or ingress/egress.

### Prior convictions may be proved to affect credibility

- **id**: rsmo-491-050
- **cite**: RSMo 491.050
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1981; current 2026-09-30.

491.050. Convicts competent witnesses — convictions and certain pleas may be proved to affect credibility. — Any person who has been convicted of a crime is, notwithstanding, a competent witness; however, any prior criminal convictions may be proved to affect his credibility in a civil or criminal case and, further, any prior pleas of guilty, pleas of nolo contendere, and findings of guilty may be proved to affect his credibility in a criminal case. Such proof may be either by the record or by his own cross-examination, upon which he must answer any question relevant to that inquiry, and the party cross-examining shall not be concluded by his answer.

- **summary**: A person's prior convictions (and certain pleas/findings) may be proved to affect credibility, by the record or by cross-examination.
**annotations**

#### State v. Vorhees, 248 S.W.3d 585 (Mo. banc 2008)

- **court**: Mo. Sup. Ct. (en banc)
- **year**: 2008
- **holding**: General rule against proof of other crimes (separate from credibility impeachment).
- **verification**: existence-only
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 47 as of 2026-09-30); earlier pipeline (03-verification-report) reports reading the full text, not re-read in this task | Read in this task: general rule that proof of separate and distinct crimes is inadmissible unless it has a legitimate tendency to establish guilt of the charged offense; the five-exception list not re-read.


- **related**: RSMo 546.260; Rule 27.05
- **definedTerms**: impeachment by prior conviction
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Revisor annotations (not read as opinions): impeachment by arrest is reversible error (1974); 'conviction' excludes a suspended imposition of sentence finding (M.A.B. v. Nicely, 1995); overruling a pretrial motion limiting cross on convictions was not error (1976). The alleged 2023 'harassment conviction' appears only in an officer's PC statement and is unverified.; Re-verified 2026-10-05 on revisor.mo.gov: "any prior criminal convictions may be proved to affect his credibility in a civil or criminal case and, further, any prior pleas of guilty, pleas of nolo contendere, and findings of guilty may be proved to affect his credibility in a criminal case." Consequence for 26MR-CR00032-01: the 2023 SIS plea can likely be used to impeach Tyler if he testifies, even though an SIS is not a conviction.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=491.050
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30; re-checked 2026-10-05

### Defendant may testify; cross-examination

- **id**: rsmo-546-260
- **cite**: RSMo 546.260
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 7/19/1985; current 2026-09-30.

546.260. Defendant may testify in own behalf — spouse may testify for husband or wife — spouse may testify against husband or wife, when. — 1. No person shall be incompetent to testify as a witness in any criminal cause or prosecution by reason of being the person on trial or examination, or by reason of being the husband or wife of the accused, but any such facts may be shown for the purpose of affecting the credibility of such witness; provided, that no person on trial or examination, nor wife or husband of such person, shall be required to testify, but any such person may testify at his or her option either on behalf of or against the defendant, and shall be liable to cross-examination, as to any matter referred to in his examination in chief, and may be contradicted and impeached as any other witness in the case; provided, that in no case shall husband or wife, when testifying under the provisions of this section, be permitted to disclose confidential communications had or made between them in the relation of such husband and wife.


A criminal defendant may testify at his or her option; if he or she does, he or she is liable to cross-examination and may be contradicted and impeached like any other witness.

- **related**: RSMo 491.050; Rule 27.05
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Decision D-2 (whether Tyler testifies) belongs to Tyler. Testifying exposes him to cross on prior convictions (491.050) and, in parallel civil cases, his statements can be used in other proceedings.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=546.260
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Certified records of courts to be evidence

- **id**: rsmo-490-130
- **cite**: RSMo 490.130
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2001; current 2026-09-30.

490.130. Certified records of courts to be evidence. — The records of judicial proceedings of any court of the United States, or of any state, attested by the clerk thereof, with the seal of the court annexed, if there be a seal, and certified by the judge, chief justice or presiding associate circuit judge of the court to be attested in due form, shall have such faith and credit given to them in this state as they would have at the place whence the said records come. Copies from the record of proceedings of any court of this state, attested by the clerk thereof, with the seal of the court annexed, if there be a seal, or if there be no seal, with the private seal of the clerk, shall be received as evidence of the acts or proceedings of such court in any court of this state. Records of proceedings of any court of this state contained within any statewide court automated record-keeping system established by the supreme court shall be received as evidence of the acts or proceedings in any court of this state without further certification of the clerk, provided that the location from which such records are obtained is disclosed to the opposing party.

- **summary**: Certified records of judicial proceedings are evidence; useful for proving Pike County order 25-AFOP-24 and the vacatur in Marion County.
- **related**: RSMo 455.040
- **definedTerms**: certified copy
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472; 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: Obtain the certified March 2025 order and docket; AI-transcribed audio is not a court record and should not be quoted in filings.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=490.130
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Venue for nontort and tort suits (incl. 90-day deemed-granted rule, .10)

- **id**: rsmo-508-010
- **cite**: RSMo 508.010
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2019 (3 histories; A.L. 2019 S.B. 7); current 2026-09-30. Applies to the 7/9/2026 (26PI-CC00044) and 8/26/2026 (26PI-CC00053) filings.

2. In all actions in which there is no count alleging a tort, venue shall be determined as follows: [...] 4. Notwithstanding any other provision of law, in all actions in which there is any count alleging a tort and in which the plaintiff was first injured in the state of Missouri, venue shall be in the county where the plaintiff was first injured by the acts or conduct alleged in the action. [...] 8. In any action for defamation or for invasion of privacy, the plaintiff shall be considered first injured in the county in which the defamation or invasion was first published. [...] 9. In all actions, venue shall be determined as of the date the plaintiff was first injured. [...] 10. All motions to dismiss or to transfer based upon a claim of improper venue shall be deemed granted if not denied within ninety days of filing of the motion unless such time period is waived in writing by all parties. [...] 14. A plaintiff is considered first injured where the trauma or exposure occurred rather than where symptoms are first manifested. [...] 15. If the county where the plaintiff's claim is filed is not a proper venue, that plaintiff shall be transferred to a county where proper venue can be established. If no such county exists in the state of Missouri, the claim shall be dismissed without prejudice. [...] 16. Denial of a motion to transfer venue pursuant to section* 507.040, 507.050, or 508.010, if denied in error, requires reversal, and no finding of prejudice under Missouri supreme court rule 84.13(b) is required for reversal.


For any suit with a tort count where the plaintiff was first injured in Missouri, venue lies in the county where the plaintiff was first injured (.4), determined as of the first-injury date (.9), where 'first injured' means where the trauma or exposure occurred (.14). Defamation and invasion of privacy: first injured where first published (.8). Non-tort suits: generally the defendant's county (.2). A motion to dismiss or transfer for improper venue is DEEMED GRANTED if not denied within 90 days of filing unless all parties waive in writing (.10). If the filing county is improper the claim must be transferred to a proper county, or dismissed without prejudice if none exists in Missouri (.15). Denial of a transfer motion in error requires reversal without a prejudice finding (.16).

- **elements**: Venue facts, not elements: (1) any tort count? (2) was plaintiff first injured in Missouri? (3) where did the trauma or exposure occur (.14)? (4) for defamation, where first published (.8)? (5) for non-tort counts, where do defendants reside (.2)?

### Five-year limitations period (contracts, statutory liabilities, goods/chattels, fraud)

- **id**: rsmo-516-120
- **cite**: RSMo 516.120
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1939 (unchanged); current 2026-09-30.

(1) All actions upon contracts, obligations or liabilities, express or implied, except those mentioned in section 516.110, and except upon judgments or decrees of a court of record, and except where a different time is herein limited; [...] (2) An action upon a liability created by a statute other than a penalty or forfeiture; [...] (4) An action for taking, detaining or injuring any goods or chattels, including actions for the recovery of specific personal property, or for any other injury to the person or rights of another, not arising on contract and not herein otherwise enumerated; [...] (5) An action for relief on the ground of fraud, the cause of action in such case to be deemed not to have accrued until the discovery by the aggrieved party, at any time within ten years, of the facts constituting the fraud.


Five years for contract/obligation actions; actions on a liability created by statute (other than penalty/forfeiture); taking, detaining or injuring goods or chattels, including recovery of specific personal property, and other injuries to the person or rights of another not arising on contract; and fraud (accrual on discovery, within ten years).

### Definitions for chapters 304 and 307 (incl. 'abandoned property', 'towing company')

- **id**: rsmo-304-001
- **cite**: RSMo 304.001
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2021 (3 histories); current 2026-09-30.

(1) "Abandoned property", any unattended motor vehicle, trailer, all-terrain vehicle, outboard motor or vessel removed or subject to removal from public or private property as provided in sections 304.155 and 304.157, whether or not operational. For any vehicle towed from the scene of an accident at the request of law enforcement and not retrieved by the vehicle's owner within five days of the accident, the agency requesting the tow shall be required to write an abandoned property report or a crime inquiry and inspection report; [...] (14) "Towing company", any person or entity which tows, removes or stores abandoned property;


'Abandoned property' means any unattended motor vehicle removed or subject to removal from public or private property under 304.155 and 304.157, whether or not operational; a vehicle towed from an accident scene at law enforcement's request and not retrieved by the owner within five days triggers a duty on the requesting agency to write an abandoned property report or crime inquiry and inspection report.

- **related**: RSMo 304.155; RSMo 304.156; RSMo 304.157
- **definedTerms**: abandoned property; towing company
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00053
- **cautions**: The 5-day accident-tow sentence is a REPORTING duty for the agency, not a definition of abandonment for every purpose. Heartland released the Jeep on day 4 (12/8/2025 after a 12/4/2025 wreck) - inside the five days - so the abandoned-property machinery may never have been triggered.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=304.001
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Removal of abandoned property from public property; tow operator's lien

- **id**: rsmo-304-155
- **cite**: RSMo 304.155
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2009 (4 histories); current 2026-09-30.

1. Any law enforcement officer within the officer's jurisdiction, or an officer of a government agency where that agency's real property is concerned, may authorize a towing company to remove to a place of safety: [...] (5) Any abandoned property for which the person operating such property is arrested for an alleged offense for which the officer takes the person into custody and where such person is unable to arrange for the property's timely removal; [...] 9. Any person who removes abandoned property at the direction of a law enforcement officer or an officer of a government agency where that agency's real property is concerned as provided in this section shall have a lien for all reasonable charges for the towing and storage of the abandoned property until possession of the abandoned property is voluntarily relinquished to the owner of the abandoned property or to the holder of a valid security interest of record. Any personal property within the abandoned property need not be released to the owner thereof until the reasonable or agreed charges for such recovery, transportation or safekeeping have been paid or satisfactory arrangements for payment have been made, except that any medication prescribed by a physician shall be released to the owner thereof upon request. The company holding or storing the abandoned property shall either release the personal property to the owner of the abandoned property or allow the owner to inspect the property and provide an itemized receipt for the contents. The company holding or storing the property shall be strictly liable for the condition and safe return of the personal property. Such lien shall be enforced in the manner provided under section 304.156.


Lists when a law enforcement officer may authorize a tow of 'abandoned property' (unattended on right-of-way, illegally left, stolen, operator arrested, etc.) and requires a crime inquiry and inspection report. Subsection 9 gives a tow operator a lien for reasonable towing and storage charges 'until possession of the abandoned property is voluntarily relinquished to the owner ... or to the holder of a valid security interest of record.'

- **elements**: Tow authorized by a law enforcement officer under subsection 1; towing company then has the subsection 9 lien until voluntary relinquishment to the owner or a secured party.
**annotations**

- **year**: 2009

- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 5 as of 2026-09-30)

Amond concerned refusal to release to the owner.; Amond cites the lien as 304.155.9 under an earlier statutory numbering; the current subsection number was verified (9) in this task.; Penalty and MMPA cross-reference live in 304.158.11 (not quoted here; earlier pipeline verified), not in 304.156.

### Notice to towing company, owner or lienholder; storage charges; owner's petition; title transfer

- **id**: rsmo-304-156
- **cite**: RSMo 304.156
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 1/1/2005 (3 histories, see footnote); current 2026-09-30.

1. Within five working days of receipt of the crime inquiry and inspection report under section 304.155 or the abandoned property report under section 304.157, the director of revenue shall search the records of the department of revenue, or initiate an inquiry with another state, if the evidence presented indicated the abandoned property was registered or titled in another state, to determine the name and address of the owner and lienholder, if any. After ascertaining the name and address of the owner and lienholder, if any, the department shall, within fifteen working days, notify the towing company. Any towing company which comes into possession of abandoned property pursuant to section 304.155 or 304.157 and who claims a lien for recovering, towing or storing abandoned property shall give notice to the title owner and to all persons claiming a lien thereon, as disclosed by the records of the department of revenue or of a corresponding agency in any other state. The towing company shall notify the owner and any lienholder within ten business days of the date of mailing indicated on the notice sent by the department of revenue, by certified mail, return receipt requested. [...] 2. A towing company may only assess reasonable storage charges for abandoned property towed without the consent of the owner. Reasonable storage charges shall not exceed the charges for vehicles which have been towed with the consent of the owner on a negotiated basis. Storage charges may be assessed only for the time in which the towing company complies with the procedural requirements of sections 304.155 to 304.158. [...] 5. (1) The owner of the abandoned property removed pursuant to the provisions of section 304.155 or 304.157 or any person claiming a lien, other than the towing company, within ten days after the receipt of notification from the towing company pursuant to subsection 1 of this section may file a petition in the associate circuit court in the county where the abandoned property is stored to determine if the abandoned property was wrongfully taken or withheld from the owner. The petition shall name the towing company among the defendants. The petition may also name the agency ordering the tow or the owner, lessee or agent of the real property from which the abandoned property was removed. The director of revenue shall not be a party to such petition but a copy of the petition shall be served on the director of revenue who shall not issue title to such abandoned property pursuant to this section until the petition is finally decided.


The Department of Revenue searches ownership records within 5 working days and, within 15 working days, notifies the towing company. A towing company that comes into possession of abandoned property under 304.155/304.157 and claims a lien must notify the title owner and lienholders by certified mail, return receipt requested, within TEN BUSINESS DAYS of the date of mailing on the DOR notice, with eight required notice contents. Storage charges may be assessed only for the time the company complies with the procedural requirements. The owner may, within ten days of receiving the notice, petition the associate circuit court to decide whether the property was wrongfully taken or withheld; after 30 days from mailing, an unredeemed vehicle can be titled to the lienholder. The text contains no private right of action, damages clause or penalty.

- **elements**: Applies only to 'abandoned property' towed under 304.155 or 304.157.; Towing company claims a lien and must notify owner and lienholders within ten business days of DOR's mailing date.; Notice must contain the eight listed statements (facility, date/reason/place of removal, charges accrue, lien claimed, right to retake, right to hearing, 30-day title-transfer warning, excess-charge liability).
**annotations**

#### Amond v. Ron York & Sons Towing, 302 S.W.3d 708 (Mo. App. E.D. 2009)

- **court**: Mo. Ct. App. E.D.
- **year**: 2009
- **holding**: Noncompliance with 304.156 notice requirements defeated the towing company's right to retain and amounted to conversion (text read).
- **verification**: full-text
- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 5 as of 2026-09-30)

(2) The statute reaches only 'abandoned property' towed under 304.155/304.157; a consensual/driver-called wrecker tow may be outside it.; Negligence per se fit is weak: element 3 (injury of the kind the statute prevents) and element 4 (proximate cause: release to Lake, not a missing notice, caused the loss). ### Vehicles left unattended on private property of another: removal procedure

- **id**: rsmo-304-157
- **cite**: RSMo 304.157
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2008 (4 histories); current 2026-09-30.

1. If a person abandons property, as defined in section 304.001, on any real property owned by another without the consent of the owner or person in possession of the property, at the request of the person in possession of the real property, any member of the state highway patrol, state water patrol*, sheriff, or other law enforcement officer within his jurisdiction may authorize a towing company to remove such abandoned property from the property in the following circumstances: [...] (1) The abandoned property is left unattended for more than forty-eight hours; or [...] 4. The owner of real property or lessee in lawful possession of the real property or the property or security manager of the real property may authorize a towing company to remove abandoned property or property parked in a restricted or assigned area without authorization by a law enforcement officer only when the owner, lessee or property or security manager of the real property is present. A property or security manager must be a full-time employee of a business entity. An authorization to tow pursuant to this subsection may be made only under any of the following circumstances: [...] 6. Any towing company which tows abandoned property without authorization from a law enforcement officer pursuant to subsection 4 of this section shall deliver a copy of the abandoned property report to the local law enforcement agency having jurisdiction over the location from which the abandoned property was towed. The copy may be produced and sent by facsimile machine or other device which produces a near exact likeness of the print and signatures required, but only if the law enforcement agency receiving the report has the technological capability of receiving such copy and has registered the towing company for such purpose. The registration requirements shall not apply to law enforcement agencies located in counties of the third or fourth classification. The report shall be delivered within two hours if the tow was made from a signed location pursuant to subdivision (1) of subsection 4 of this section, otherwise the report shall be delivered within twenty-four hours.


Governs tows of property abandoned on another's private real property. At the possessor's request a law enforcement officer may authorize a tow when the property is unattended for more than 48 hours or is a safety hazard or unreasonably interferes with use; the owner/lessee/property manager may authorize a tow without an officer only when present and in listed circumstances; a towing company that tows without law-enforcement authorization must deliver the abandoned property report to local law enforcement. Incorporated by reference into 304.156's notice duty.

- **related**: RSMo 304.155; RSMo 304.156; RSMo 304.001
- **definedTerms**: abandoned property; towing company
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00053
- **cautions**: Only subsections 1, 4 and 6 and the list of report contents were read closely; subsections 2, 3, 5, 7-9 (including penalty language) were not read in full.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=304.157
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

(4) "Merchandise", any objects, wares, goods, commodities, intangibles, real estate or services; [...] (5) "Person", any natural person or his legal representative, partnership, firm, for-profit or not-for-profit corporation, whether domestic or foreign, company, foundation, trust, business entity or association, and any agent, employee, salesman, partner, officer, director, member, stockholder, associate, trustee or cestui que trust thereof; [...] (7) "Trade" or "commerce", the advertising, offering for sale, sale, or distribution, or any combination thereof, of any services and any property, tangible or intangible, real, personal, or mixed, and any other article, commodity, or thing of value wherever situated. ### Unlawful merchandising practices

- **id**: rsmo-407-020
- **cite**: RSMo 407.020
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2020 (5 histories); current 2026-09-30. Only subsections 1 (first sentence set) and 2 were read in full.

1. The act, use or employment by any person of any deception, fraud, false pretense, false promise, misrepresentation, unfair practice or the concealment, suppression, or omission of any material fact in connection with the sale or advertisement of any merchandise in trade or commerce or the solicitation of any funds for any charitable purpose, as defined in section 407.453, in or from the state of Missouri, is declared to be an unlawful practice. The use by any person, in connection with the sale or advertisement of any merchandise in trade or commerce or the solicitation of any funds for any charitable purpose, as defined in section 407.453, in or from the state of Missouri of the fact that the attorney general has approved any filing required by this chapter as the approval, sanction or endorsement of any activity, project or action of such person, is declared to be an unlawful practice.


Declares unlawful any deception, fraud, false pretense, false promise, misrepresentation, unfair practice, or concealment/suppression/omission of a material fact 'in connection with the sale or advertisement of any merchandise in trade or commerce,' whether committed before, during or after the sale. Willful violation with intent to defraud is a class E felony (subsection 3).

- **elements**: Act, use or employment of a deception, fraud, false pretense, false promise, misrepresentation, unfair practice or concealment of a material fact;; in connection with the sale or advertisement of merchandise in trade or commerce (before, during or after the sale).

### MMPA private civil action; damages; fees

- **id**: rsmo-407-025
- **cite**: RSMo 407.025
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2020 (2 histories; S.B. 591). Applies to cases filed after 8/28/2020.

1. (1) Any person who purchases or leases merchandise primarily for personal, family or household purposes and thereby suffers an ascertainable loss of money or property, real or personal, as a result of the use or employment by another person of a method, act or practice declared unlawful by section 407.020, may bring a private civil action in either the circuit court of the county in which the seller or lessor resides or in which the transaction complained of took place, to recover actual damages. [...] (2) A person seeking to recover damages shall establish: [...] 2. The court may, in its discretion: [...] 4. A cause of action under this section accrues on the date of purchase or lease described in subsection 1 of this section or upon receipt of notice of a method, act, or practice declared unlawful by section 407.020.


A person who purchases or leases merchandise primarily for personal, family or household purposes and suffers an ascertainable loss of money or property as a result of an unlawful practice may sue for actual damages in the circuit court of the county where the seller/lessor resides or the transaction took place. The plaintiff must show reasonable-consumer conduct, that the practice would cause a reasonable person to enter the transaction, and definite, objective damages. The court may award punitive damages, attorney's fees to the prevailing party, and equitable relief.

- **elements**: Purchase or lease of merchandise primarily for personal, family or household purposes.; Ascertainable loss of money or property as a result of the unlawful practice.; Plaintiff acted as a reasonable consumer; the practice would cause a reasonable person to enter the transaction; individual damages proven with definitive, objective evidence.
**annotations**

### Computation of time under the civil code

- **id**: rsmo-506-060
- **cite**: RSMo 506.060
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2003; current 2026-09-30.

1. In computing any period of time prescribed or allowed by this code, by order of court, or by any applicable statute, the day of the act, event, or default after which the designated period of time begins to run is not to be included. The last day of the period so computed is to be included, unless it is a Saturday, Sunday, or a legal holiday, in which event the period runs until the end of the next day which is neither a Saturday, Sunday, nor a legal holiday. When the period of time prescribed or allowed is less than seven days,


Statutory counterpart of Rule 44.01(a): exclude the day of the event; include the last day unless it is a Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday; periods under seven days exclude intermediate weekends/holidays. Subsection 4 requires a written motion and notice of hearing to be served at least five days before the hearing.

- **related**: Rule 44.01; RSMo 508.010
- **definedTerms**: computation of time
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053; 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: Covers periods 'prescribed ... by any applicable statute' (as does Rule 44.01(a)), but no case was located applying the weekend roll to the 90 days in 508.010.10 - treat day 90 as the literal date.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=506.060
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Notice of appeal; transcript charges; consequences of not taking further steps

- **id**: rsmo-512-050
- **cite**: RSMo 512.050
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1997; current 2026-09-30.

All charges due to the court reporter for preparation of the transcript of the record of the trial court shall be paid within ten days of the ordering of the transcript. In the event that actual charges due for the preparation of the transcript cannot be readily determined, a deposit in the amount of the estimated charges due for preparation of the transcript shall be paid within ten days of the written notification by the court reporter of the amount of such estimated charges. The court reporter shall provide such written notification within ten days of any request for transcript. After a timely filing of such notice of appeal, failure of the appellant to take any of the further steps to secure the review of the judgment or order appealed from does not affect the validity of the appeal, but is ground for such action as the appellate court deems appropriate,


Transcript charges are due within ten days of ordering (or an estimate deposit within ten days of the reporter's written notification); after a timely notice of appeal, failure to take further steps does not affect the appeal's validity but is ground for such action as the appellate court deems appropriate, including dismissal.

- **related**: RSMo 512.130; Rule 81.12; Rule 81.19; Rule 81.20
- **definedTerms**: notice of appeal; transcript; record on appeal
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: Says nothing about in forma pauperis relief; Tyler's $700 transcript deposit IFP was granted 9/8/2026 and the transcript ordered 9/9/2026 (well after the ten-day order requirement in Rule 81.12(c)(1)). Explain the delay honestly (8/25 deposit demand; 9/1 IFP motion).
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=512.050
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Transcript on appeal: when filed

- **id**: rsmo-512-130
- **cite**: RSMo 512.130
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1943; current 2026-09-30.

512.130. Transcript, when filed. — The transcript on appeal shall be filed with, and the action transferred to, the appellate court within ninety days from the date of filing of the notice of appeal, except that, when more than one appeal is taken from the same judgment or order to the same appellate court, the trial court may prescribe the time for filing and transferring, which in no event shall be less than ninety days from the date of filing of the first notice of appeal.


The transcript on appeal is to be filed with the appellate court within ninety days from the filing of the notice of appeal (same 90 days as Rule 81.19(b)); the statute contains no extension mechanism - Rule 81.20 supplies it.

- **related**: Rule 81.19; Rule 81.20; RSMo 512.050
- **definedTerms**: record on appeal

### MHRA: unlawful employment practices

- **id**: rsmo-213-055
- **cite**: RSMo 213.055
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2017 (2 histories; A.L. 2017 S.B. 43); current 2026-09-30. Termination 5/9/2026.

1. It shall be an unlawful employment practice: [...] (a) To fail or refuse to hire or to discharge any individual, or otherwise to discriminate against any individual with respect to his compensation, terms, conditions, or privileges of employment, because of such individual's race, color, religion, national origin, sex, ancestry, age or disability;


Makes it an unlawful employment practice for an employer, because of race, color, religion, national origin, sex, ancestry, age or disability, to discharge or otherwise discriminate against an individual with respect to compensation, terms, conditions or privileges of employment. (The page does not list a retaliation provision; retaliation is in 213.070, not read.)

- **elements**: Employer; adverse employment action (discharge or discrimination in terms/conditions); because of a protected characteristic (here, disability) - causation standard in 213.111.5: protected classification must be the 'direct proximate cause' ('because of') of the claimed damages.
- **related**: RSMo 213.075; RSMo 213.111; 42 U.S.C. 12112; 29 U.S.C. 2615
- **definedTerms**: unlawful employment practice; disability (MHRA)
- **matters**: EEOC 560-2026-03085
- **cautions**: Tyler's employer separated him 5/9/2026 for alleged 'dishonesty'; no retaliation/FMLA theory arises under 213.055 itself.; 213.111.5 puts the burden on the plaintiff to prove the decision was made 'because of' the protected classification and was the direct proximate cause of damages (2017 amendment, S.B. 43) - a stricter causation rule than the pre-2017 'contributing factor' standard; earlier MHRA cases in the revisor annotations predate it.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=213.055
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### MHRA: filing a complaint (180-day jurisdictional deadline); EEOC dual-filing

- **id**: rsmo-213-075
- **cite**: RSMo 213.075
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2017 (2 histories; S.B. 43); current 2026-09-30.

As a jurisdictional condition precedent to filing a civil action under this chapter, any person claiming to be aggrieved by an unlawful discriminatory practice shall make, sign and file with the commission a verified complaint in writing, within one hundred eighty days of the alleged act of discrimination [...] 2. Any complaint which is filed with the federal Equal Employment Opportunity Commission or other federal agencies with which the commission has a work-sharing or deferral agreement, or with a local commission which has been certified as substantially equivalent by the commission, shall be deemed filed with the commission on the date that such complaint is received by such federal agency or local commission. A copy of all complaints filed with a local commission with the authority to enforce the provisions of this chapter is to be forwarded to the commission within seven days of the filing thereof with such local commission. If a local commission has jurisdiction to hear a complaint filed with the commission, such complaint shall be deemed to have been filed with the local commission on the date on which such complaint was filed with the commission. The commission shall, within seven days of the receipt of a complaint which a local commission has jurisdiction to hear, forward a copy thereof to such local commission.


As a 'jurisdictional condition precedent' to a civil action, an aggrieved person must file a verified written complaint with the Missouri Commission on Human Rights within 180 days of the alleged act of discrimination; failure deprives the commission of jurisdiction and is a complete defense that can be raised at any time. A complaint filed with the EEOC (or another agency with a work-sharing or deferral agreement) is deemed filed with the commission on the date the federal agency received it (subsection 2).

- **elements**: Verified written complaint filed with MCHR (or deemed filed via EEOC work-sharing) within 180 days of the alleged act of discrimination.
**annotations**

### MHRA: right to civil action; relief; damages caps; causation

- **id**: rsmo-213-111
- **cite**: RSMo 213.111
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/2017 (2 histories; S.B. 43); current 2026-09-30.

Any action brought in court under this section shall be filed within ninety days from the date of the commission's notification letter to the individual but no later than two years after the alleged cause occurred or its reasonable discovery by the alleged injured party. [...] 2. The court may grant as relief, as it deems appropriate, any permanent or temporary injunction, temporary restraining order, or other order, and may award to the plaintiff actual and punitive damages, and may award court costs and reasonable attorney fees to the prevailing party, other than a state agency or commission or a local commission; except that, a prevailing respondent may be awarded reasonable attorney fees only upon a showing that the case was without foundation. [...] 4. The sum of the amount of actual damages, including damages for future pecuniary losses, emotional pain, suffering, inconvenience, mental anguish, loss of enjoyment of life, and other nonpecuniary losses, and punitive damages awarded under this section shall not exceed for each complaining party: [...] 5. In any employment-related civil action brought under this chapter, the plaintiff shall bear the burden of proving the alleged unlawful decision or action was made or taken because of his or her protected classification and was the direct proximate cause of the claimed damages.


After 180 days from filing, if the commission has not completed processing and the person asks in writing, the commission issues a right-to-sue letter; suit must be filed within 90 days of the letter and no later than two years after the alleged cause occurred or its reasonable discovery. Relief includes injunction, actual and punitive damages, costs and fees (a prevailing respondent gets fees only if the case was 'without foundation'); jury trial; capped damages by employer size; plaintiff bears the burden of proving the decision was made 'because of' the protected classification and was the direct proximate cause of damages.

- **elements**: Timely MCHR complaint (213.075); right-to-sue letter; suit within 90 days of letter and within two years of the cause; proof that the protected classification was the direct proximate cause (213.111.5).
**annotations**

- **holding**: Revisor annotation: right-to-sue letter not jurisdictional but a condition precedent (pre-2017).

- **treatment**: not checked (CourtListener has no citator; cited-by count 40 as of 2026-09-30)

### Presumption of paternity; rebuttal

- **id**: rsmo-210-822
- **cite**: RSMo 210.822
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 8/28/1998; current 2026-09-30.

1. A man shall be presumed to be the natural father of a child if: [...] (4) An expert concludes that the blood tests show that the alleged parent is not excluded and that the probability of paternity is ninety-eight percent or higher, using a prior probability of 0.5. [...] 2. A presumption pursuant to this section may be rebutted in an appropriate action only by clear and convincing evidence, except that a presumption under subsection 1 of this section that arises from a blood test or the filing of an acknowledgment of paternity in a state or territory in which the blood test or the filing creates a conclusive presumption by law also has conclusive effect in Missouri. If two or more presumptions arise which conflict with each other, the presumption which on the facts is founded on the weightier considerations of policy and logic controls. The presumption is rebutted by a court decree establishing the paternity of the child by another man.


A man is presumed to be the natural father if he and the mother were married when the child was born (or within 300 days after), attempted marriage, married after birth and acknowledged/named/obliged to support, or a genetic test expert concludes he is not excluded with 98% or higher probability. Presumptions are rebutted only by clear and convincing evidence; a court decree of paternity by another man also rebuts.

- **related**: RSMo 210.823; RSMo 210.826; RSMo 210.830; RSMo 210.834
- **definedTerms**: presumed father; clear and convincing evidence
- **matters**: 26PI-FC00048
- **cautions**: Whether any presumption arises for Tyler (marriage, acknowledgment, birth-certificate listing, support order) is unknown from the record; the 3/11/2025 consent order pleaded a 'child in common'.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=210.822
- **retrieved**: 2026-09-30

### Acknowledgment of paternity: legal finding; rescission

- **id**: rsmo-210-823
- **cite**: RSMo 210.823
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Version effective 7/1/1997 (see footnote); current 2026-09-30.

1. A signed acknowledgment of paternity form pursuant to section 193.215 shall be considered a legal finding of paternity subject to the right of either signatory to rescind the acknowledgment, in writing, by filing such rescission with the bureau within the earlier of:


A signed acknowledgment of paternity is a legal finding of paternity, rescindable by filing with the bureau within 60 days of the last signature (or the date of a proceeding to establish support); later challenge only for fraud, duress or material mistake of fact, burden on the challenger.

- **related**: RSMo 210.822; RSMo 210.826
- **definedTerms**: acknowledgment of paternity
- **matters**: 26PI-FC00048
- **cautions**: Whether Tyler ever signed an acknowledgment is unverified; if he did, genetic testing alone may not be enough after the 60 days.
