### Service of pleadings and other papers

- **id**: rule-43-01
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 43.01
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: July 1, 2018.

(a) Service - When Required. Each party shall be served with:
(1) Every pleading subsequent to the original petition;
(2) Every written motion, other than one that may be heard ex parte; and
(3) Every written notice, appearance, demand, offer of judgment, order, and similar paper that by statute, court rule or order is required to be served.
No service need be made on parties in default for failure to appear, except that pleadings asserting new or additional claims for relief against them shall be served upon them in the manner provided for service of summons. In cases filed under Chapter 517 RSMo, service as required in Rule 43.01 shall be made on every party that has entered or appeared in person or by attorney. [...] (c) Service - How and by Whom Made. Attorneys and self-represented parties shall state in the signature blocks of their pleadings their current mailing addresses, telephone numbers, facsimile numbers, electronic addresses, and Missouri bar numbers if any. This information shall be kept current at all times. Service may be directed to any of these addresses.
Unless otherwise ordered by the court, service required by Rules 43.01(a) and 43.01(b) may be made in the following manner:
(1) Upon the attorney:
(A) By delivering a copy to the attorney;
(B) By leaving a copy at the attorneys office with a clerk, receptionist, or secretary or with an attorney employed by or associated with the attorney to be served;
(C) By facsimile transmission;
(D) By electronic mail; or
(E) By mailing a copy to the attorney at the attorneys last known address;
(2) Upon a party:
(A) By delivering or mailing a copy to the party;
(B) By facsimile transmission;
(C) By electronic mail; or
(D) By serving a copy in the manner provided for service of summons in Rule 54.13.


Sets who must be served, how, and when service is complete; self-represented parties must state mailing address, telephone, fax, e-mail in their signature block; certificates of service must state the name served, date, method and address.

- **related**: Rule 44.01; Rule 59.01(c)(3)
- **definedTerms**: service; certificate of service
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053; 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: The mirror shows a version revised July 1, 2018; later e-filing rule changes (Rule 103) were not read, so the completion-of-service details for e-filed papers are unconfirmed.; ED114714: H.G. is protected by an order barring communication 'in any manner or through any medium'; Tyler mailing/e-mailing service of an appellate motion to H.G. could itself be a contact - use a non-party adult or ask the ED Clerk (earlier pipeline).; Text read from the Clerk Handbook mirror of the Supreme Court Rules on courts.mo.gov (official site, but a mirror that may lag the main rules page; main page blocked to automation). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.

### Criminal rules: time computation; enlargement; motions; mail

- **id**: rule-20-01
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 20.01
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: Janaury 1, 1980.

(a) Computation. In computing any period of time prescribed or allowed by these Rules, by order of court, or by any applicable statute, the day of the act, event, or default after which the designated period of time begins to run is not to be included. The last day of the period so computed is to be included, unless it is a Saturday, Sunday, or a legal holiday, in which event the period runs until the end of the next day which is neither a Saturday, Sunday nor a legal holiday. When the period of time prescribed or allowed is less than seven days, intermediate Saturdays, Sundays, and legal holidays shall be excluded in the computation. [...] (d) Motions - Notice Required - Affidavits. A written motion, other than one which may be heard ex parte, and notice of the hearing thereof shall be served not later than five days before the time specified for the hearing, unless a different period is fixed by law or court rule or by order of the court. Such an order may for cause shown be made on ex parte application. When a motion is supported by an affidavit, the affidavit shall be served with the motion; and, except as otherwise provided by law or rule in connection with a motion for a new trial, opposing affidavits may be served not later than one day before the hearing, unless the court permits them to be served at some other time.


Criminal counterpart of Rule 44.01: same computation; the court may not enlarge the period for a new-trial motion or appeal; motions and notice of hearing must be served five days before the hearing.

- **related**: Rule 44.01; RSMo 545.780
- **definedTerms**: computation of time (criminal)
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Use Rule 20.01, not 44.01, for 26MR-CR00032 and -00472 deadlines. The revised-date line on the mirror reads 'Janaury 1, 1980' (typo in source).; Text read from the Clerk Handbook mirror of the Supreme Court Rules on courts.mo.gov (official site, but a mirror that may lag the main rules page; main page blocked to automation). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.

### Criminal rules: pretrial conference

- **id**: rule-24-12
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 24.12
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: January 1, 1980.

At any time after the filing of the indictment or information the court upon motion of any party or upon its own motion may order one or more conferences to consider such matters as will promote a fair and expeditious trial. The court shall make a record of the matters agreed upon. No admission made by the defendant or his attorney at a conference shall be used against the defendant unless the admissions are reduced to writing and signed by the defendant and his attorney. This Rule shall not be invoked unless the defendant is represented by counsel.


The court may order pretrial conferences to promote a fair and expeditious trial; admissions by defendant or counsel cannot be used unless reduced to writing and signed; 'This Rule shall not be invoked unless the defendant is represented by counsel.'

- **related**: RSMo 545.780; Rule 25.03
- **definedTerms**: pretrial conference
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Tyler is pro se: by its terms Rule 24.12 shall not be invoked unless the defendant is represented by counsel, so the 10/2/2026 'pretrial conference' may be a local/court-set setting rather than a Rule 24.12 conference. Confirm the basis with the court. The written-and-signed-admission protection also does not apply to a pro se defendant in words.; Text read from the Clerk Handbook mirror of the Supreme Court Rules on courts.mo.gov (official site, but a mirror that may lag the main rules page; main page blocked to automation). ### Criminal rules: amendment of information

- **id**: rule-23-08
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 23.08
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: January 1, 2003.

Any information may be amended or an information may be substituted for an indictment at any time before verdict or finding if:
(a) No additional or different offense is charged, and
(b) A defendant's substantial rights are not thereby prejudiced.
No such amendment or substitution shall cause delay of a trial unless the court finds that a defendant needs further time to prepare a defense by reason of such amendment or substitution.


An information may be amended any time before verdict if no additional or different offense is charged and the defendant's substantial rights are not prejudiced; an amendment must not cause trial delay unless the court finds the defendant needs more time.

- **related**: RSMo 455.085; Rule 23.04 (title only)
- **definedTerms**: amendment of information
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00472; 26MR-CR00032

### Criminal rules: disclosure by State without court order

- **id**: rule-25-03
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 25.03
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: July 1, 2022.

(a) Disclosure upon filing of felony complaint. Except as otherwise provided in these Rules, the state shall, upon written request of defendant's counsel, disclose to defendant's counsel the following material and information in the possession of the prosecutor: any arrest reports, incident reports, investigative reports, written or recorded statements, documents, photographs, video, electronic communications and electronic data that relate to the offense for which defendant is charged. [...] (b) Disclosure after indictment or filing of information. Except as otherwise provided in these Rules, the state shall, upon written request of defendant's counsel, disclose to defendant's counsel the following material and information within its possession or control designated in the request:
(1) Any arrest reports, incident reports, investigative reports, written or recorded statements, documents, photographs, video, electronic communications and electronic data that relate to the offense for which defendant is charged;
(2) The names and last known addresses of persons whom the state intends to call as witnesses at any hearing or at the trial, together with their written or recorded statements, and existing memoranda, reporting or summarizing part or all of their oral statements;


On written request of defendant's counsel, the State must disclose arrest/incident/investigative reports, recorded statements, video, electronic data, names and addresses of witnesses it intends to call with their statements, and the other listed material in its possession or control.

### Criminal rules: failure of defendant or spouse to testify

- **id**: rule-27-05
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 27.05
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Revised/effective date shown on the mirror: July 1, 2023.

(a) If the defendant shall not avail himself or herself of his or her right to testify, or of the testimony of the wife or husband, on the trial in the case, it shall not be construed to affect the innocence or guilt of the accused, nor shall the same raise any presumption of guilt, nor be referred to by any attorney in the case, nor be considered by the court or jury before whom the trial takes place.
(b) If the defendant does not testify, and the defendant so requests, but not otherwise, the court shall instruct the jury in writing as follows:
"Under the law, a defendant has the right not to testify. No presumption of guilt may be raised and no inference of any kind may be drawn from the fact that defendant did not testify."


If the defendant does not testify, no presumption of guilt or inference may be drawn, and on the defendant's request the court instructs the jury in writing that a defendant has the right not to testify.

- **related**: RSMo 546.260; RSMo 491.050
- **definedTerms**: right not to testify
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: 'Rule 27.40(b)' (cited in the case file for speedy trial) DOES NOT EXIST: the mirror's Rule 27 lists 27.01 through 27.26 only.; Text read from the Clerk Handbook mirror of the Supreme Court Rules on courts.mo.gov (official site, but a mirror that may lag the main rules page; main page blocked to automation). ### Civil action for deprivation of rights

- **id**: usc-42-1983
- **cite**: 42 U.S.C. 1983
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30 (no amendment date captured).

Every person who, under color of any statute, ordinance, regulation, custom, or usage, of any State or Territory or the District of Columbia, subjects, or causes to be subjected, any citizen of the United States or other person within the jurisdiction thereof to the deprivation of any rights, privileges, or immunities secured by the Constitution and laws, shall be liable to the party injured in an action at law, suit in equity, or other proper proceeding for redress, except that in any action brought against a judicial officer for an act or omission taken in such officer’s judicial capacity, injunctive relief shall not be granted unless a declaratory decree was violated or declaratory relief was unavailable. For the purposes of this section, any Act of Congress applicable exclusively to the District of Columbia shall be considered to be a statute of the District of Columbia.


Creates a damages/equitable action against any person who, under color of state law, deprives another of rights secured by the Constitution and laws. It supplies no substantive rights itself; the claim is the underlying right (e.g., Fourth Amendment false arrest, unlawful search, excessive force). Injunctive relief against a judicial officer for judicial acts is limited. Missouri borrows the five-year period of RSMo 516.120(4) for 1983 claims.

- **elements**: Conduct by a person acting under color of state law;; deprivation of a right secured by the Constitution or federal law;; causation and damages; for a local government, an official policy or custom was the moving force (Monell), which may be a single decision by a final policymaker (Pembaur);; defendant-specific immunities: absolute (prosecutors, Imbler) and qualified (Harlow/Pearson).

### FMLA: prohibited acts (interference and retaliation)

- **id**: usc-29-2615
- **cite**: 29 U.S.C. 2615
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30 (Pub. L. 103-3, 1993).

It shall be unlawful for any employer to interfere with, restrain, or deny the exercise of or the attempt to exercise, any right provided under this subchapter. [...] It shall be unlawful for any employer to discharge or in any other manner discriminate against any individual for opposing any practice made unlawful by this subchapter. [...] (b) Interference with proceedings or inquiriesIt shall be unlawful for any person to discharge or in any other manner discriminate against any individual because such individual


Makes it unlawful for an employer to interfere with, restrain or deny the exercise of FMLA rights (interference, (a)(1)); to discharge or discriminate against an individual for opposing a practice made unlawful by the subchapter ((a)(2)); and for any person to discriminate against an individual for filing a charge, instituting a proceeding, or giving information or testimony under it ((b)).

- **elements**: Eligible employee (12 months; 1,250 hours; worksite with 50 employees within 75 miles; 29 U.S.C. 2611(2)) - not read as a separate entry;; entitlement to leave for a qualifying reason (serious health condition, 2611(11));; notice to employer; interference/denial of a benefit or retaliation because of protected activity;; prejudice/damages (2617).

### FMLA: enforcement; damages; limitations

- **id**: usc-29-2617
- **cite**: 29 U.S.C. 2617
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30.

Any employer who violates section 2615 of this title shall be liable to any eligible employee affected— [...] The court in such an action shall, in addition to any judgment awarded to the plaintiff, allow a reasonable attorney’s fee, reasonable expert witness fees, and other costs of the action to be paid by the defendant. [...] Except as provided in paragraph (2), an action may be brought under this section not later than 2 years after the date of the last event constituting the alleged violation for which the action is brought. [...] In the case of such action brought for a willful violation of section 2615 of this title, such action may be brought within 3 years of the date of the last event constituting the alleged violation for which such action is brought.


An employer that violates section 2615 is liable to the affected eligible employee for lost wages/benefits (or actual monetary losses up to 12 weeks' pay), interest, and an equal amount as liquidated damages (reducible only for good faith and reasonable grounds), plus equitable relief including reinstatement; reasonable attorney's fees, expert fees and costs are mandatory for a prevailing plaintiff. Suit may be brought in federal or state court within 2 years of the last event constituting the violation, or 3 years for a willful violation.

- **elements**: Violation of 2615; employee affected; damages/equitable relief; filing within 2 years (3 if willful) of the last event.
- **related**: 29 U.S.C. 2615
- **definedTerms**: liquidated damages; willful violation
- **matters**: EEOC 560-2026-03085
- **cautions**: Case-file dates: termination 5/9/2026 -> 2-year deadline 5/9/2028; 3-year willful 5/9/2029. 'The last event constituting the alleged violation' may not be the termination if earlier denial of leave is alleged.; FMLA claims are not brought through the EEOC; they can be filed directly in court.
- **source**: https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/29/2617 ; Text read from Cornell LII (law.cornell.edu), an unofficial reproduction of the U.S. Code; the official uscode.house.gov page was reachable but was not parsed. Confirm the current text and any post-2025 amendment on uscode.house.gov.

### ADA Title I: discrimination

- **id**: usc-42-12112
- **cite**: 42 U.S.C. 12112
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30.

No covered entity shall discriminate against a qualified individual on the basis of disability in regard to job application procedures, the hiring, advancement, or discharge of employees, employee compensation, job training, and other terms, conditions, and privileges of employment.


No covered employer may discriminate against a qualified individual on the basis of disability in hiring, advancement, discharge, compensation, training and other terms, conditions and privileges of employment. 'Discriminate' includes limiting or classifying employees adversely and, in the construction clause (not quoted here), failing to provide reasonable accommodation absent undue hardship.

- **elements**: Covered entity; disability (42 U.S.C. 12102: impairment that substantially limits a major life activity, a record of it, or being regarded as having one, excluding transitory and minor impairments of 6 months or less for the 'regarded as' prong);; qualified individual (can perform essential functions with or without reasonable accommodation);; adverse action because of disability.

### Title VII/ADA: time for filing a charge; right-to-sue

- **id**: usc-42-2000e-5-e-1
- **cite**: 42 U.S.C. 2000e-5(e)(1)
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30; incorporated into the ADA by 42 U.S.C. 12117(a).

A charge under this section shall be filed within one hundred and eighty days after the alleged unlawful employment practice occurred and notice of the charge (including the date, place and circumstances of the alleged unlawful employment practice) shall be served upon the person against whom such charge is made within ten days thereafter, except that in a case of an unlawful employment practice with respect to which the person aggrieved has initially instituted proceedings with a State or local agency with authority to grant or seek relief from such practice or to institute criminal proceedings with respect thereto upon receiving notice thereof, such charge shall be filed by or on behalf of the person aggrieved within three hundred days after the alleged unlawful employment practice occurred, or within thirty days after receiving notice that the State or local agency has terminated the proceedings under the State or local law, whichever is earlier, and a copy of such charge shall be filed by the Commission with the State or local agency. [...] (f)(1) If a charge filed with the Commission pursuant to subsection (b), is dismissed by the Commission, or if within one hundred and eighty days from the filing of such charge or the expiration of any period of reference under subsection (c) or (d), whichever is later, the Commission has not filed a civil action under this section or the Attorney General has not filed a civil action in a case involving a government, governmental agency, or political subdivision, or the Commission has not entered into a conciliation agreement to which the person aggrieved is a party, the Commission, or the Attorney General in a case involving a government, governmental agency, or political subdivision, shall so notify the person aggrieved and within ninety days after the giving of such notice a civil action may be brought against the respondent named in the charge


A charge must be filed within 180 days after the unlawful employment practice occurred, extended to 300 days (or 30 days after notice that the state/local agency terminated proceedings, if earlier) where the person first instituted proceedings with a state or local agency with authority to grant or seek relief. Notice of the charge goes to the respondent within ten days. After dismissal or 180 days without EEOC action, the EEOC notifies the person, who then has 90 days to sue (subsection (f)(1)).

- **elements**: Charge filed within 180 days (300 in a deferral state / work-sharing context);; notice of right to sue; suit within 90 days of notice.

- **id**: usc-42-12117
- **cite**: 42 U.S.C. 12117(a)

The powers, remedies, and procedures set forth in sections 2000e–4, 2000e–5, 2000e–6, 2000e–8, and 2000e–9 of this title shall be the powers, remedies, and procedures this subchapter provides to the Commission, to the Attorney General, or to any person alleging discrimination on the basis of disability in violation of any provision of this chapter, or regulations promulgated under section 12116 of this title, concerning employment.

### Proceedings in forma pauperis

- **id**: usc-28-1915
- **cite**: 28 U.S.C. 1915
- **type**: federal
- **inForce**: Current Code text as posted by LII 2026-09-30.

Subject to subsection (b), any court of the United States may authorize the commencement, prosecution or defense of any suit, action or proceeding, civil or criminal, or appeal therein, without prepayment of fees or security therefor, by a person who submits an affidavit that includes a statement of all assets such prisoner possesses that the person is unable to pay such fees or give security therefor. Such affidavit shall state the nature of the action, defense or appeal and affiant’s belief that the person is entitled to redress. [...] The court may request an attorney to represent any person unable to afford counsel. [...] (2) Notwithstanding any filing fee, or any portion thereof, that may have been paid, the court shall dismiss the case at any time if the court determines that— (A) the allegation of poverty is untrue; or (B) the action or appeal— (i) is frivolous or malicious; (ii) fails to state a claim on which relief may be granted; or (iii) seeks monetary relief against a defendant who is immune from such relief.


A federal court may allow a person who submits an affidavit of inability to pay to proceed without prepaying fees. The court may request an attorney for a person unable to afford counsel. Regardless of fees paid, the court must dismiss at any time if the poverty allegation is untrue or the action is frivolous or malicious, fails to state a claim, or seeks damages from an immune defendant.

- **elements**: Affidavit of inability to pay (and, for prisoners, the prisoner-specific requirements in (a)(2) and (b)); screening under (e)(2).

### Criminal rules: continuing duty to disclose

- **id**: rule-25-08
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 25.08
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Adopted June 13, 1979, eff. Jan. 1, 1980; amended Dec. 19, 2017, eff. July 1, 2018 (per state-rules.com, read 2026-10-05).

If after complying with a request for disclosure or order of court, a party discovers information or material that the party would have been required to disclose under the request or order, the party shall furnish this additional information or material to opposing counsel as soon as practicable. If the additions are discovered during trial, the court also shall be notified.


Discovery is not one-and-done. Anything found later that should have been disclosed must be turned over as soon as practicable, and the court must be told if it surfaces during trial.

- **elements**: Earlier request or court order; Party later discovers covered material; Must furnish it as soon as practicable; notify the court if during trial
- **related**: Rule 25.03; Rule 25.18
- **definedTerms**: discovery (criminal)
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting in a filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/25.08/
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Bill of particulars

- **id**: rule-23-04
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 23.04
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective Jan. 1, 2003 (courts.mo.gov page 199260, read 2026-10-05).

When an indictment or information alleges the essential facts constituting the offense charged but fails to inform the defendant of the particulars of the offense sufficiently to prepare a defense, the court may direct or permit the filing of a bill of particulars. A motion for a bill of particulars may be made before arraignment or within ten days after arraignment or at such later time as the court may permit. A bill of particulars may be amended at any time subject to such conditions as justice requires.


If the charge doesn't tell the defendant enough to prepare a defense, the court can order the State to spell out the details. The motion is due within 10 days after arraignment, or later with the court's permission.

- **elements**: Information alleges the essential facts; But fails to give particulars sufficient to prepare a defense; Motion before arraignment, within 10 days after, or later with leave
- **related**: Rule 24.04; Rule 23.08 (amendment; not read)
- **definedTerms**: bill of particulars; information
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: 26MR-CR00472: arraigned 8/28/2026, so the 10-day window has passed. The Oct 5 motion asks leave under 'at such later time as the court may permit.'
- **source**: https://www.courts.mo.gov/page.jsp?id=199260
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Pretrial motions: defenses and objections

- **id**: rule-24-04
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 24.04
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Current text as read 2026-10-05 (state-rules.com).

(b)(1) Any defense or objection which is capable of determination without trial of the general issue may be raised before trial by motion. [...] (b)(2) Defenses and objections based on defects in the institution of the prosecution or in the indictment or information other than that it fails to show jurisdiction in the court or to charge an offense may be raised only by motion before trial. [...] (b)(4) The motion shall be heard and determined before trial on application of the state or the defendant, unless the court orders that the hearing and determination thereof be deferred until the trial.


Lets a defendant raise, before trial, any defense that can be decided without a trial on guilt. Defects in the charging document must be raised before trial, and the motion must include all such objections then available. The court decides the motion before trial unless it chooses to defer it to trial.

- **elements**: Defense capable of determination without trial of the general issue (b)(1); Charging-document defects must be raised before trial (b)(2), all at once; Heard before trial unless deferred (b)(4)
**annotations**

The 'no full order in effect' ground is a (b)(1) element defense, not a pleading defect.; Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting.

### Business records admitted by custodian affidavit

- **id**: rsmo-490-692
- **cite**: RSMo 490.692
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Current revisor.mo.gov text as read 2026-10-05.

1. Any records or copies of records reproduced in the ordinary course of business by any photographic, photostatic, microfilm, microcard, miniature photographic, optical disk imaging, or other process which accurately reproduces or forms a durable medium for so reproducing the original [...] 2. [records and affidavit must be served on all other parties] at least seven days prior to the day upon which trial of the cause commences [...] 3. [Form:] THE STATE OF ______ COUNTY OF ______ AFFIDAVIT. Before me, the undersigned authority, personally appeared ______, who, being by me duly sworn, deposed as follows: My name is ______, I am of sound mind, capable of making this affidavit, and personally acquainted with the facts herein stated: I am the custodian of the records of ______. Attached hereto are ______ pages of records from ______. These ______ pages of records are kept by ______ in the regular course of business, and it was the regular course of business of ______ for an employee or representative of ______ with knowledge of the act, event, condition, opinion, or diagnosis recorded to make the record or to transmit information thereof to be included in such record; and the record was made at or near the time of the act, event, condition, opinion or diagnosis. The records attached hereto are the original or exact duplicates of the original. [Affiant; notary jurat with seal.]


Business records (like a hospital chart) come into evidence on a custodian's sworn affidavit instead of live testimony, if the records and affidavit are served on every other party at least seven days before trial starts.

- **elements**: Records kept in the regular course of business, made at or near the time; Custodian affidavit in the subsection 3 form; Served on all parties at least 7 days before trial begins
- **related**: RSMo 490.680 (not read); RSMo 191.227
- **definedTerms**: business records; custodian of records
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032
- **cautions**: 26MR-CR00032-01: trial Wed 11/4/2026, so the last day to serve is Wed 10/28/2026. Internal target is 10/21.; Serving the records gives them to the State. Review the ER chart before deciding to use it.; Subsections 1-2 were captured partly paraphrased (bracketed). Quote only the bracket-free text, or re-read the page first.

### Patient's right to copies of medical records; fees

- **id**: rsmo-191-227
- **cite**: RSMo 191.227
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Current revisor.mo.gov text as read 2026-10-05. Fee amounts are adjusted annually.

1. All physicians, chiropractors, hospitals, dentists, and other duly licensed practitioners in this state, herein called "providers", shall, upon written request of a patient, or guardian or legally authorized representative of a patient, furnish a copy of his or her record of that patient's health history and treatment rendered to the person submitting a written request [...] within a reasonable time of the receipt of the request [...]


A hospital must give a patient a copy of their own records within a reasonable time after a written request. Subsection 2 caps fees for search/retrieval, per-page copying, electronic delivery and postage (amounts indexed yearly), and allows a notary fee of no more than $2.

- **elements**: Written request by the patient or legal representative; Provider furnishes a copy within a reasonable time; Fees limited by subsection 2
- **related**: RSMo 490.692; 45 C.F.R. 164.524 (HIPAA right of access; not read)
- **definedTerms**: medical records
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032
- **cautions**: Dollar figures in subsection 2 change every year; do not quote amounts without re-reading.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=191.227
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Involuntary dismissal

- **id**: rule-67-03
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 67.03
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Adopted Jan. 19, 1973, eff. Sept. 1, 1973; amended June 1, 1993, eff. Jan. 1, 1994.

A defendant may move for an involuntary dismissal of the civil action for lack of jurisdiction, for prematurity of action, for failure to substitute a party for a decedent and for such other dismissals as are allowed by these Rules 41 through 101. Defendant may also move for an involuntary dismissal of the civil action for failure of the plaintiff to prosecute or to comply with these Rules 41 through 101 or any order of the court. Any involuntary dismissal shall be without prejudice unless the court in its order for dismissal shall otherwise specify.

- **summary**: A dismissal the plaintiff didn't ask for is without prejudice (you can refile) unless the order says otherwise.
**annotations**

#### Chromalloy American Corp. v. Elyria Foundry Co., 955 S.W.2d 1, 3-4 (Mo. banc 1997)

- **court**: Mo. Sup. Ct.
- **year**: 1997
- **holding**: A dismissal without prejudice is generally not a final, appealable judgment, unless it effectively bars refiling in the same court.
- **verification**: full-text


- **related**: Rule 67.06; Rule 67.02; RSMo 516.230
- **definedTerms**: dismissal without prejudice
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044
- **cautions**: Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting in a filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/67.03/
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Leave to amend after a motion to dismiss is sustained

- **id**: rule-67-06
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 67.06
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Current text as read 2026-10-05.

On sustaining a motion to dismiss a claim, counterclaim or cross-claim the court shall freely grant leave to amend and shall specify the time within which the amendment shall be made or amended pleading filed. If the amended pleading is not filed within the time allowed, final judgment of dismissal with prejudice shall be entered on motion except in cases of excusable neglect; in which cases amendment shall be made promptly by the party in default.


When the court grants a motion to dismiss, it should freely let the plaintiff amend and set a deadline. Miss that deadline and the dismissal can become with prejudice.

- **related**: Rule 75.01; Rule 67.03
- **definedTerms**: leave to amend
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044
- **cautions**: 26PI-CC00044: if the 10/5/2026 order gave no leave to amend, a motion within 30 days of entry (Rule 75.01) can ask for it.; Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting in a filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/67.06/
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Voluntary dismissal

- **id**: rule-67-02
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 67.02
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Current text as read 2026-10-05.

(a) [...] a civil action may be dismissed by the plaintiff without order of the court anytime: (1) Prior to the swearing of the jury panel for the voir dire examination, or (2) In cases tried without a jury, prior to the introduction of evidence at the trial. [...] (c) A voluntary dismissal under Rule 67.02(a) shall be without prejudice unless otherwise specified by the plaintiff.

### Trial court's 30-day control over judgments

- **id**: rule-75-01
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 75.01
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Amended June 10, 1980, eff. Jan. 1, 1981; amended Oct. 1, 2024, eff. July 1, 2025.

Except as otherwise provided in Rule 81.045, the trial court retains control over judgments during the 30-day period after entry of judgment and may, after giving the parties an opportunity to be heard and for good cause, vacate, reopen, correct, amend, or modify its judgment within that time. [...]

- **summary**: For 30 days after entry, the judge can vacate or change the judgment for good cause after hearing the parties.
- **related**: Rule 81.05; Rule 67.06
- **definedTerms**: entry of judgment
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044

### What counts as a judgment

- **id**: rule-74-01
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 74.01(a)
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Current text as read 2026-10-05.

"Judgment" as used in these rules includes a decree and any order from which an appeal lies. A judgment is rendered when entered. A judgment is entered when a writing signed by the judge and denominated "judgment" or "decree" is filed. [...]

- **summary**: Only a signed writing labeled 'judgment' or 'decree' (a separate paper or a docket entry) starts appeal clocks.
- **related**: Rule 81.05; Rule 81.04

### When a civil judgment becomes final

- **id**: rule-81-05
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.05(a)
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Current text as read 2026-10-05.

(1) A judgment becomes final in a civil matter at the expiration of 30 days after its entry if no timely authorized after-trial motion is filed. (2) If a party timely files an authorized after-trial motion, the judgment becomes final at the earlier of the following: (A) 90 days from the date the last timely motion was filed, on which date all motions not ruled shall be deemed overruled; or (B) If all motions have been ruled, then the date of ruling of the last motion to be ruled or 30 days after entry of judgment, whichever is later.

- **summary**: With no after-trial motion, a judgment is final 30 days after entry. With one, it's final when the last motion is ruled (but not before day 30) or at day 90.
- **related**: Rule 81.04; Rule 75.01
- **definedTerms**: final judgment
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting in a filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/81.05/
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Pleading a claim (fact pleading; tort prayer)

- **id**: rule-55-05
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 55.05
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Most recently amended eff. Jan. 1, 1995 (per mirror).

A pleading that sets forth a claim for relief, whether an original claim, counterclaim, cross-claim, or third-party claim shall contain (1) a short and plain statement of the facts showing that the pleader is entitled to relief and (2) a demand for judgment for the relief to which the pleader claims to be entitled. [...] [In tort actions for damages the prayer asks for] such damages as are fair and reasonable [...]


Missouri requires facts, not conclusions, showing each element. In tort damage claims the prayer must not state a dollar amount; it asks for 'such damages as are fair and reasonable.'

- **elements**: Short and plain statement of FACTS showing entitlement to relief; Demand for judgment; Tort damages: no dollar figure in the prayer
- **related**: Rule 55.27
- **definedTerms**: fact pleading
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053; 26MR-CV00879
- **cautions**: Bracketed parts were summarized by the page reader. Re-read before quoting.; Text read from state-rules.com (unofficial mirror). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before quoting in a filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/55.05/
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Savings statute: new action within one year after a nonsuit

- **id**: rsmo-516-230
- **cite**: RSMo 516.230
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Effective Aug. 28, 1939; current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-10-05.

If any action shall have been commenced within the times respectively prescribed in sections 516.010 to 516.370, and the plaintiff therein suffer a nonsuit, or, after a verdict for him, the judgment be arrested, or, after a judgment for him, the same be reversed on appeal or error, such plaintiff may commence a new action from time to time, within one year after such nonsuit suffered or such judgment arrested or reversed; [...]


If a timely-filed case is dismissed without a decision on the merits (a nonsuit), the plaintiff gets one more year to refile, even if the limitation period has run in the meantime.

- **elements**: Original action filed within the limitation period; Nonsuit (e.g., dismissal without prejudice); New action within one year
- **related**: RSMo 516.120; Rule 67.03
- **definedTerms**: nonsuit; savings statute
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044
- **cautions**: 26PI-CC00044: the window runs one year from the dismissal's entry (about 10/5/2027 if entered 10/5/2026). Recompute from the actual entry date.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=516.230
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Transfer-on-death (beneficiary) designation on a vehicle title

- **id**: rsmo-301-681
- **cite**: RSMo 301.681
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Effective Aug. 28, 2004; current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-10-05.

1. A sole owner of a motor vehicle or trailer, and multiple owners [...] may request the director of revenue to issue a certificate of ownership for the motor vehicle or trailer in beneficiary form [...] 3. A certificate of ownership issued in beneficiary form shall include after the name of the owner, or after the names of multiple owners, the words 'transfer on death to' or the abbreviation 'TOD' followed by the name of the beneficiary or beneficiaries. 4. (1) During the lifetime of a sole owner or prior to the death of the last surviving multiple owner, the signature or consent of the beneficiary or beneficiaries shall not be required for any transaction relating to the motor vehicle or trailer for which a certificate of ownership in beneficiary form has been issued. [...] 5. (1) On proof of death of one of the owners of two or more multiple owners, or of a sole owner, surrender of the outstanding certificate of ownership [...] the director of revenue shall issue a new certificate of ownership [...] to the surviving beneficiary or beneficiaries.


Lets an owner name a TOD beneficiary on the title. The beneficiary takes ownership only after the owner dies; during the owner's life the beneficiary's signature or consent isn't needed for any transaction.

- **related**: RSMo 301.210
- **definedTerms**: TOD; beneficiary form
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053; 26RL-CR00078
- **cautions**: Applied 10/5/2026: Tyler says Griffin was named TOD on the Jeep title. That gave her no ownership or authority to sell during his lifetime. Confirm the TOD line on the DOR title history (Form 5091 request).; Subsection 4(2)-(4) were summarized by the page reader, not quoted.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=301.681
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Assignment of title on sale; sale without assignment presumed void

- **id**: rsmo-301-210
- **cite**: RSMo 301.210
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Effective Aug. 28, 2020; current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-10-05.

1. In the event of a sale or transfer of ownership of a motor vehicle or trailer for which a certificate of ownership has been issued, the holder of such certificate shall endorse on the same an assignment thereof, with warranty of title in form printed thereon [...] 4. It shall be unlawful for any person to buy or sell in this state any motor vehicle or trailer registered under the laws of this state, unless, at the time of the delivery thereof, there shall pass between the parties such certificates of ownership with an assignment thereof, as provided in this section, and the sale of any motor vehicle or trailer registered under the laws of this state, without the assignment of such certificate of ownership, shall be presumed fraudulent and void unless the parties have executed a written agreement for delayed delivery of certificate of ownership as provided in subsection 5 of this section.


Only the title holder can assign the title. A sale of a Missouri-registered vehicle without the title and a proper assignment passing between the parties is unlawful and presumed fraudulent and void.

- **elements**: Certificate holder endorses the assignment; Certificate with assignment passes at delivery; Otherwise the sale is presumed fraudulent and void (subsection 4)
- **related**: RSMo 301.681
- **definedTerms**: certificate of ownership; assignment of title
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 26PI-CC00053; 26RL-CR00078
- **cautions**: Defense risk: a title signed in blank and left in the glove box may be argued as consent or estoppel. Plead permission to drive only; TOD status confirms ownership stayed with Tyler.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=301.210
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-05

### Prior inconsistent statements are substantive evidence in criminal trials

- **id**: rsmo-491-074
- **cite**: RSMo 491.074
- **type**: statute
- **inForce**: Effective Aug. 28, 2000 (L. 1985 H.B. 366; A.L. 2000 S.B. 757 & 602); current on revisor.mo.gov 2026-10-06.

Notwithstanding any other provisions of law to the contrary, a prior inconsistent statement of any witness testifying in the trial of a criminal offense shall be received as substantive evidence, and the party offering the prior inconsistent statement may argue the truth of such statement.


In a criminal trial, a testifying witness's earlier statement that conflicts with their trial testimony comes in as evidence of what happened, not just to attack credibility, and the offering party may argue it is true.

- **elements**: The witness testifies at a criminal trial; The prior statement is inconsistent with that testimony; It is then admissible as substantive evidence
- **related**: RSMo 491.050
- **definedTerms**: prior inconsistent statement; substantive evidence
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032
- **cautions**: Applied 10/6/2026: on Sgt.'s camera T211268 at about 15:16:37, Griffin's first answer was 'he resisted and you guys had to put him to the ground,' before the sergeant asked if she saw him 'slam himself on the ground.' If she testifies he threw himself down, the earlier answer may be offered under this section. Confirm the words by listening; the machine transcripts disagreed on the first word.; Laying the foundation (time, place, person present, the statement itself) is still required. Case law on foundation was not read today.
- **source**: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=491.074
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Late notice of appeal by special order

- **id**: rule-81-07
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.07
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective date shown on the mirror: January 1, 2007.

(a) Appeal by Special Order – Motion – Notice. When an appeal is permitted by law from a judgment in the trial court, but the time prescribed for filing an ordinary notice of appeal with the clerk of the trial court as set forth in Rule 81.04 has expired, a party may seek a special order of the appropriate appellate court permitting a late filing of the notice of appeal. The special order may be allowed by the appellate court only upon motion with notice to the adverse parties filed within six months from the date the judgment appealed from became final for purposes of appeal and only upon a showing by affidavit, or otherwise, that the delay was not due to appellant's culpable negligence. A copy of the judgment from which the appeal is sought shall be attached to any such motion. When notified of the issuance of a special order by the appellate court, the clerk of the trial court in which the judgment was entered shall permit the appellant to file a notice of appeal within ten days after such notification, or such other time as specified in the order, and the appellant shall then proceed to prepare the record on appeal as if the appeal had been allowed without a special order. [...]


If the 10-day notice-of-appeal deadline (Rule 81.04) is missed, the appellate court may allow a late notice by special order. The motion must be filed within six months after the judgment became final, with notice to the other side, a copy of the judgment attached, and an affidavit (or other showing) that the delay was not due to the appellant’s culpable negligence. After the order, the notice of appeal is filed within ten days.

- **elements**: Judgment appealable by law; Rule 81.04 deadline has passed; Motion in the appellate court within six months of finality, with notice to adverse parties; Copy of the judgment attached; Showing that the delay was not the appellant’s culpable negligence
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.04; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.05(a)
- **matters**: 26PI-CC00044; 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: The six months run from the date the judgment became final (Rule 81.05), not from entry.; “Culpable negligence” is the test; a reason supported by affidavit is the safest showing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/81.07/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Form of briefs; word limits; certificate of compliance

- **id**: rule-84-06
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.06
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Last amended effective January 1, 2024 (per mirror).

Appellant's initial brief and all briefs in a cross appeal except the reply brief shall not exceed 31,000 words. [respondent’s brief 27,900 words; reply brief 7,750 words; certificate of compliance required — quoted fragments only, rest summarized]


Sets the statewide brief format and word limits (appellant 31,000; respondent 27,900; reply 7,750) and requires a certificate of compliance stating the word count (word-processor count may be relied on) and how the brief was served. Cover, certificates, signature block and appendix are not counted.

- **elements**: Brief conforms to Rule 84.03(b) format; Within the word limit; Certificate of compliance with word count and service information
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.04; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.05; E.D. Special Rule 360
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: In the Eastern District, Special Rule 360 sets LOWER limits (15,500 / 13,950 / 3,875 words). Use the Eastern District limits for ED114714.; Only fragments of this rule’s text were captured; read the full rule before filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/84.06/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Involuntary dismissal of appeal; dismissal docket notice

- **id**: rule-84-08
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.08
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective January 1, 2017 (as amended), per mirror.

(a) After the timely filing of a notice of appeal, if the appellant fails to take the further steps required to secure review of the appeal within the periods of time allowed or as extended, the clerk shall place the case on a dismissal docket. The clerk shall notify all parties that the appeal will be dismissed unless the appellant remedies the default before a specified date. The date shall not be less than 15 days from the date of the notice. If the default is not remedied by that date, an order of dismissal shall be entered. (b) If the party who is aggrieved by an agency decision fails to file the first brief as required under Rule 84.05, that party shall be given notice as otherwise required in this Rule 84.08. If the party fails to remedy the default, any party aggrieved by the circuit court decision may file a motion in the appellate court requesting that the judgment of the circuit court be vacated.


Missing a step after the notice of appeal (record, brief) puts the case on the dismissal docket. The clerk sends notice giving at least 15 days to cure; if not cured, the appeal is dismissed.

- **elements**: Timely notice of appeal; Missed later step (record, brief) within allowed or extended time; Clerk’s notice with cure date at least 15 days out; Dismissal if not cured
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.19; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.20; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.05
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: A dismissal-docket notice is a hard deadline. Cure the default (or get an extension under Rule 81.20 / 84.05(a)) before the date in the notice.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/84.08/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Redaction requirements (appellate and civil filings)

- **id**: rule-84-015
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.015
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Adopted June 28, 2022, eff. July 1, 2023; amended Dec. 16, 2025, eff. July 1, 2026.

(a) Redaction of Confidential Information. Unless otherwise ordered by the court, any counsel, party, or other person offering any filing that is not a confidential record, as defined in Court Operating Rule 4.24(a), shall redact confidential information, as defined in Court Operating Rule 4.24(b), in the filing. [...] (b) Responsibility for Redaction. For all filings in any court, the responsibility for redacting confidential information rests solely with the counsel, parties, or any other person offering the filing. Courts will not review each filing to ensure compliance and cannot refuse to accept a filing on that basis. (c) Manner of Redaction. All redactions shall be done in a manner that makes it clear that information has been redacted. [...] When a filer redacts information from a filing offered in any court, the filer also must contemporaneously file either: (1) a confidential unredacted version of the filing; or (2) a confidential redacted information filing sheet that sets out the information redacted from the filing with an explanation as to where the information was redacted in the filing or the generic descriptors used in the filing to reference the redacted information. (d) Compliance Certification. When offering a filing, all filers shall affirmatively certify compliance with the redaction requirements in Rules 19.10, 55.025, and 84.015. [...] by a paper form attached to the filing or on the filing itself. [...] (g) Transcripts of Open Court Proceedings. Any transcript of an open court proceeding offered for filing in any court is not subject to the redaction requirements. [...]


Filers (not the court) must redact confidential information defined in Court Operating Rule 4.24(b), make the redaction visible, file either an unredacted confidential copy or a redacted-information sheet, and certify compliance (paper filers do it on a form or on the filing). Open-court transcripts are exempt when filed.

- **elements**: Identify confidential information under COR 4.24(b); Redact visibly; File unredacted confidential copy or redaction sheet; Certify compliance (paper form if not an e-filer)
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 19.10; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.026
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714; 26PI-CC00044
- **cautions**: Court Operating Rule 4.24 (what counts as confidential) was not read here — check it before filing.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/84.015/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Service of filings in appellate courts

- **id**: rule-84-026
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.026
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Adopted Aug. 15, 2023, effective July 1, 2024.

(a) Initial Filings. The electronic filing system does not serve initial filings on parties. For any new case filed in the electronic filing system in this Court or the court of appeals, including but not limited to initial filings pursuant to Rules 81.07, 83.01, 83.04, 84.035, and 84.24, service of the initially filed documents shall be made pursuant to Rule 43.01 with a certificate of service. (b) Subsequent Filings. For filings on an existing case, registered users serve and receive service of all filings through the electronic filing system; no additional service or certificate of service is required. (c) Parties Ineligible to Use the Electronic Filing System. If any party is ineligible to file documents through the electronic filing system, service after the initial filing shall be made as follows: (1) Each registered user shall serve documents as provided by Rule 43.01 on every ineligible party; (2) Each ineligible party shall serve documents as provided by Rule 43.01 on every party not represented by an attorney. Service of the documents need not be made on parties represented by an attorney; the notice generated by the electronic filing system shall constitute service on such parties.


Initial appellate filings (including a Rule 81.07 motion or a writ petition) must be served under Rule 43.01 with a certificate of service. Later filings serve through e-filing. A party who cannot e-file must serve unrepresented parties under Rule 43.01, but need not separately serve parties who have lawyers.

- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 43.01; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.07; E.D. Special Rule 330
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: If you file on paper/email under E.D. Rule 330, you still must serve any unrepresented opposing party yourself under Rule 43.01.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/civil/84.026/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Redaction requirements (criminal filings)

- **id**: rule-19-10
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 19.10
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Adopted June 28, 2022, eff. July 1, 2023; amended Dec. 16, 2025, eff. July 1, 2026.

[quoted fragments] “the responsibility for redacting confidential information rests solely with the counsel, parties, or any other person offering the filing.” [...] “all redactions shall be done in a manner that makes it clear that information has been redacted.”


Criminal-case counterpart of Rule 84.015: the filer redacts confidential information, files an unredacted confidential copy or a redaction sheet, and certifies compliance; open-court transcripts are exempt at filing.

- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.015
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Full text not captured; read the rule before filing exhibits that contain personal identifiers.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/19.10/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.

### Pleas (misdemeanors or felonies); no nolo contendere

- **id**: rule-24-02
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 24.02
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective July 1, 2022 (per mirror).

[quoted fragment] Pleas allowed: “not guilty, guilty, or not guilty by reason of mental disease or defect” [...] “If a defendant refuses to plead or if a corporation fails to appear, the court shall enter a plea of not guilty.” [Committee Note: “the deletion of ‘nolo contendere.’”]


Missouri allows only three pleas: not guilty, guilty, or not guilty by reason of mental disease or defect. Nolo contendere was deleted. Before accepting a guilty plea the court must personally advise the defendant (24.02(b)).

- **related**: MAI-CR 4th 408.02
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Nolo contendere is NOT available in Missouri state court (see Latin glossary).
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/24.02/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Motions to suppress evidence

- **id**: rule-24-05
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 24.05
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective January 1, 1980 (per mirror).

Requests that evidence be suppressed shall be raised by motion before trial; however, the court may in its discretion entertain a motion to suppress evidence at any time during trial.

- **summary**: A motion to suppress must be filed before trial; considering one during trial is discretionary.
- **elements**: Written motion; Filed before trial
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 24.04; 10th Cir. L.R. 67.7
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: In the 10th Circuit, Local Rule 67.7 also requires all motions to be disposed of before trial.; A pretrial ruling on suppression does not by itself preserve the issue — object at trial and include it in the motion for new trial (Rule 29.11).
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/24.05/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Defendant’s disclosure to the State without court order (incl. alibi)

- **id**: rule-25-05
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 25.05
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Amended effective July 1, 2018; adopted January 1, 1980 (per mirror).

[quoted fragment, alibi paragraph] “specific information as to the place at which defendant claims to have been at the time of the alleged offense, and as particularly as is known, the names and addresses of the witnesses”


On the State’s written request (filed and served), the defense must disclose expert reports and tests, witnesses it intends to call and their statements, physical evidence it intends to use, notice of a mental disease or defect defense, and alibi details (place, and alibi witnesses’ names and addresses).

- **related**: MAI-CR 4th 408.06; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 25.03
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Disclosure is triggered by the State’s written request — check whether one was filed in your case.

### Use of instructions and verdict forms (MAI-CR mandatory)

- **id**: rule-28-02
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 28.02
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective July 1, 1995 (per mirror).

(a) Duty of Court. In every trial for a criminal offense the court shall instruct the jury in writing upon all questions of law arising in the case that are necessary for their information in giving the verdict. (b) Request for Instructions and Verdict Forms. At the close of the evidence, or at such earlier time as the court may direct, counsel shall submit to the court instructions and verdict forms that the party requests be given. Instructions and verdict forms that a party requests shall be submitted in writing with an original and one copy for the court and one copy for each party. Each copy shall contain a notation at the end of the instruction as follows: "MAI-CR ___", "MAI-CR ___, Modified", or "Not in MAI-CR ___", as the case may be. (c) MAI-CR Excludes Use of Other Forms. Whenever there is an MAI-CR instruction or verdict form applicable under the law and Notes On Use, the MAI-CR instruction or verdict form shall be given or used to the exclusion of any other instruction or verdict form. (d) Guide for Form of Instruction Where MAI-CR Not Applicable. If an MAI-CR form must be modified or if there is no applicable MAI-CR form, the modified form or the form not in MAI-CR, if given, shall be simple, brief, impartial, and free from argument. It shall not submit detailed evidentiary facts. All instructions, where possible, shall follow the format of MAI-CR instructions, including the skeleton forms therein. (e) Procedure for Marking Instructions. At the close of the evidence the court shall call a conference of counsel for the purpose of considering instructions and verdict forms. [...] All written requests for instructions and verdict forms that are refused shall be so marked by the court, identified alphabetically, filed, and shall be kept as a part of the record of the case. The court shall dictate into the record which party requested each instruction and verdict form given or refused [...] (f) Violation of Rule – Effect. The giving or failure to give an instruction or verdict form in violation of this Rule 28.02 or any applicable Notes On Use shall constitute error, the error's prejudicial effect to be judicially determined, provided that objection has been timely made pursuant to Rule 28.03.


The applicable MAI-CR form must be used, following its Notes on Use. Requested instructions go in writing (original and one copy for the court, one copy per party), each marked “MAI-CR ___”, “Modified”, or “Not in MAI-CR”. Refused instructions are lettered and kept in the record. Deviation is error if timely objected to under Rule 28.03.

- **elements**: Written request with MAI-CR notation; Original + one copy for court + one copy per party; Refused instructions marked and filed; Objection under 28.03 to preserve
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 28.03; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 29.11; 10th Cir. L.R. 53.1
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Marion County Local Rule 53.1 requires proposed instructions, with MAI-CR numbers, AT THE PRETRIAL CONFERENCE — earlier than the statewide rule.; Make sure your refused instructions are marked and filed so the record shows what you asked for.
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/28.02/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Objections to instructions and verdict forms

- **id**: rule-28-03
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 28.03
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective July 1, 1995 (per mirror).

Counsel shall make specific objections to instructions or verdict forms considered erroneous. No party may assign as error the giving or failure to give instructions or verdict forms unless the party objects thereto before the jury retires to consider its verdict, stating distinctly the matter objected to and the grounds of the objection. Counsel need not repeat objections already made on the record prior to delivery of the instructions and verdict forms. The objections must also be raised in the motion for new trial in accordance with Rule 29.11.


To preserve an instruction error you must object specifically, state the grounds, before the jury retires — and repeat the objection in the motion for new trial.

- **elements**: Specific objection; Stated grounds; Before jury retires; Repeated in motion for new trial
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 28.02; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 29.11
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Unpreserved instruction error gets only plain-error review (see State v. Bax, Mo. App. 2015, in Case law).
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/28.03/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Motion for new trial; preservation of error

- **id**: rule-29-11
- **cite**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 29.11
- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Effective July 1, 2002 (as amended), per mirror.

[quoted fragments] A motion for new trial must be filed “within fifteen days after the return of the verdict.” [court may extend up to ten more days for good cause] [...] if the court does not rule within 90 days the motion is “denied for all purposes.”


In a jury trial, the motion for new trial is due within 15 days after the verdict (extendable once by up to 10 days on application for good cause) and must list every claimed error, except jurisdiction, whether the charge states an offense, and sufficiency of the evidence. In a bench trial it is optional. Unruled motions are denied after 90 days.

- **elements**: Filed within 15 days of verdict (+ up to 10 on good cause); Lists each error with specificity (jury trials); Affidavits for facts outside the record
- **related**: Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 28.03; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 20.01; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 30.01
- **matters**: 26MR-CR00032; 26MR-CR00472
- **cautions**: Rule 20.01 bars the court from enlarging the time for a new-trial motion beyond what 29.11 allows.; Leaving an objection out of the motion limits appeal to plain error (State v. Whittier, Mo. App. 2019).
- **source**: https://state-rules.com/missouri/criminal/29.11/ — state-rules.com (unofficial mirror of the Supreme Court Rules; courts.mo.gov blocks automated reads). Confirm on courts.mo.gov before filing.
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

- **id**: rule-30-01

- **type**: rule
- **inForce**: Most recent amendment effective January 1, 2017 (per mirror).
- **textExcerpt**: [quoted fragment] “After the rendition of final judgment in a criminal case, every party shall be entitled to any appeal permitted by law.”

Criminal appeals follow the civil notice-of-appeal procedure in Rules 81.04 and 81.08, with criminal modifications; the clerk notifies the Attorney General on felony appeals.

### Notice by appellant to clerk of court

- **id**: ed-310
- **cite**: E.D. Special Rule 310
- **type**: local
- **inForce**: Adopted Jan. 1, 1995; amended eff. Jan. 1, 2017.

Notification of any action, ruling or event which occurs subsequent to the filing of the Notice of Appeal and which affects the pending appeal shall be given to the clerk of this court by the party having knowledge of the event or the attorney of record. Examples include the death of a party, the filing of bankruptcy by a party, problems in producing the record on a timely basis, or a change in the bail bond status of a criminal defendant, including any change of address of the defendant or the surety, or a change of surety. Notice shall be filed with the clerk of this court within five days of any such action, ruling or event.


Anything after the notice of appeal that affects the appeal — including problems producing the record on time — must be reported to the Eastern District clerk within five days.

- **related**: E.D. Special Rule 340; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.20
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: Transcript delays count: notify the clerk within five days and pair it with an extension request.
- **source**: courts.mo.gov Eastern District local rules page (page.jsp?id=761)
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

### Filings by self-represented parties

- **id**: ed-330
- **cite**: E.D. Special Rule 330
- **type**: local
- **inForce**: Adopted Nov. 10, 2015; amended Jan. 1, 2017 and Feb. 14, 2017; current version effective April 13, 2021.

(a)(1) Self-represented parties may file motions, correspondence, and pleadings, including writ pleadings that do not require a filing fee, by filing a paper copy with the clerk, by facsimile transmission in accordance with subsection (b), or by e-mail attachment as provided in subsection (c) [...] (a)(4) Legal files and transcripts, including supplemental legal files and transcripts may only be filed in paper and may NOT be filed by facsimile or by e-mail attachment. [remainder summarized]


Pro se parties may file motions, letters, pleadings and briefs on paper, by fax (20 pages max) or by email to moapped@courts.mo.gov (10 MB message, 7 MB per attachment; PDF or Word for briefs). Fee-paid writ pleadings, legal files, transcripts and exhibits are paper only. Filings received by 11:59:59 p.m. are filed that day. Briefs must meet Rule 360 limits.

- **elements**: Paper, fax (≤20 pages) or email (≤10 MB; attachments ≤7 MB); Legal file and transcript: paper only; Exhibits: paper only; Briefs within Rule 360 limits
- **related**: E.D. Special Rule 360; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 84.026; Mo. Sup. Ct. R. 81.12
- **matters**: 25PI-PN00012 / ED114714
- **cautions**: The record on appeal (legal file + transcript) for ED114714 must be filed on PAPER — not by email or fax.; Keep your fax confirmation or email server receipt as proof of filing time.
- **source**: courts.mo.gov Eastern District local rules page (page.jsp?id=766)
- **retrieved**: 2026-10-06

- **id**: ed-340

An appellant seeking more time to file the record because the transcript is not done must attach a written statement from the court reporter (Form One) or, for electronically recorded proceedings, the Central Transcribing Service (Form Two). The reporter or service must prepare the statement promptly.

### Limitations on length of briefs (Eastern District)

- **id**: ed-360
- **cite**: E.D. Special Rule 360
- **type**: local
- **inForce**: Adopted eff. July 1, 2001; amended Jan. 1, 2003 and Jan. 1, 2017.

Appellant's initial brief prepared pursuant to Rule 84.05(a), an original brief filed pursuant to Rule 84.05(c), and all briefs in a cross appeal except the reply brief shall not exceed: (a) 15,500 words, or (b) 1,100 lines of text if the brief uses a monospaced face. [respondent: 90%; reply: 25%]


Eastern District word limits: appellant 15,500 words (or 1,100 monospaced lines); respondent 13,950 (990 lines); reply 3,875 (275 lines). For Rule 81.21(e) briefs: 50 / 45 / 15 pages.
